# Debug Overlay (True Values)

> Authoring guidance: .claude/docs/templates/guidance/game-design-document-guide.md (load per-section as you author — do not read entirely).

> **Status**: Designed
> **Author**: user + tools-programmer, qa-lead, creative-director (via /design-system, autonomous; orchestrated run 2026-10-26)
> **Last Updated**: 2026-10-26
> **Last Verified**: 2026-10-26
> **Creative Director Review (CD-GDD-ALIGN)**: APPROVED 2026-10-26
> **Implements Pillar**: P1 The World Never Lies (primary — the Overlay is the developer's inviolable truth channel: the one surface that reads `true_value` directly, and the instrument that proves no player-visible consequence ever disagrees with the authoritative state). **Serves**: P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality (it is how "no lie is unfalsifiable" is tested, lie by lie, and it is never an accessibility option because an honest overlay on a lying HUD would make the pillar switchable off, art bible R-7; it also gates the MVP kill/continue playtest, where an observer reads sanity stage from it, never shown to the tester); P3 The Web Watches (it is the one place Attention's level may be shown, so the world never needs a meter). **Supports**: P2 Dread Before Chaos (it displays the TellAudit result and the tell window, it implements neither)

## Summary

The Debug Overlay (True Values) is a developer-only, display-only diagnostic layer that shows the authoritative value beside the perceived value for any ref and any player, names the difference (`HONEST`, `DELIBERATE` lie, `RETURNING`, or a named `DEFECT_*` class), and surfaces the kernel's own diagnostics (`state_hash`, dirty keys against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, hook timings, the Graph's budget, Attention level, sanity per player) so that deliberate corruption can be told from real desync. It is excluded from every release build at the file level, is never an accessibility feature (art bible R-7), writes nothing, and owns the build gate, the channel registration contract and the seven MVP pages that the other systems' diagnostics plug into. Read it if you design or test anything that lies to the player, or any system that publishes a diagnostics counter.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Polish` (the systems index and design order place it last because it reads everything; the GDD template's layer list has no Polish value) · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (hard; designed)`, `Perception Filter (hard; designed in this run)`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (soft; designed)`; it also reads, as owners' published views or channels, the Life-Support Graph, Suit & Equipment, Sanity, Attention, Horror, Emitter, Lighting, Layout, Body, Assembly, Save/Load, Web Map, Interaction, Controller, Boarding, Audio and Chart Table (all designed), plus In-World Instruments (designed (this run); IW-38, P-2) and the HUD & Suit Layer (designed; its nine `hud_debug_snapshot()` counters, P-1); Accessibility, Settings, Onboarding, Co-op Session and the dev console are undesigned (provisional contracts P-3 to P-12)

## Overview

The Debug Overlay (True Values) is a **developer-only, read-only diagnostic layer**: exactly one kernel subscriber of class `DIAGNOSTIC` plus a dev-build presentation layer at pipeline layer 4, which shows, for any chosen reference and any chosen player, the authoritative value (`true_value`) beside the perceived value (`perceived`) with a named classification of the difference, and next to them the kernel's own diagnostics: `T`, kernel state, queue depth, the last-N command log, the resolved hook order with per-hook timings, `state_hash`, dirty-key counts against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, the Graph's hook budget, the Attention level and each player's sanity. Its single job is the sentence in the systems index (idx:43): to **tell deliberate corruption from real desync**. A hallucination is a lie that Sanity issued on purpose, that the Perception Filter rendered, and that carries a leak (Pillar 4: no lie is unfalsifiable). A defect is any other difference between what is true and what is shown: a stale value, a node that bypassed the Filter, a lie with no leak, a determinism break. To the player the two look identical, which is exactly why a tool that separates them is needed. The index says "authoritative values beside the *hallucinated* ones"; this GDD labels the pair **true** and **perceived**, because `Perceived` is the Filter's locked record name and an honest reading is also "perceived" (pf:247, PF-7: with no entry for the ref, `displayed` equals the true value). "Hallucinated" is index prose, not a label (orchestrator ruling: terminology reconciled, no edit to the index).

**How the player meets it: never.** The Overlay is absent from every build a player can obtain. It is excluded from release exports at the file level, not switched off at runtime (DO-2), it is not a setting, not an accessibility option, not a rebinding entry and not a tutorial line (art bible R-7, ab:886: "An honest overlay on top of a lying HUD would make Pillar 4 switchable off, and the pillar is the game"). The player meets what the tool *protects*: the promise that when a gauge and the frost beside it disagree, the frost is the ship. This is a data and infrastructure layer with a pure player-side consequence, the same register as the kernel (kern:30: "the player never meets this system; they meet a promise it keeps").

**What the game loses without it.** (1) The Perception Filter's top technical risk (idx:281: "Per-client divergence is indistinguishable from desync without tooling"; mitigation "Debug Overlay (true values) built with it"): without the tool, every "the gauge lied and I could not tell" report is unclassifiable and Pillar 1's design test cannot be run. (2) The Sanity system's QA instrument: AC-SN-88 (stage-1 entry within 268 to 363 s of boarding, sn:1626) and AC-SN-144 (named blocker "deferred: Debug Overlay", sn:1678) can only be read off this layer, and Sanity's `eligible_entries_per_min` telemetry (sn:811) replaces a reference rate with a measured one only here. (3) The MVP kill/continue playtest gate: Boarding's X-BS-11 and AC-BS-122 require an observer to read Attention `phase` "from the debug overlay and never shown to the tester" (boarding-and-salvage.md:1187, :1498), so the gate cannot run without it. (4) Six already-written acceptance criteria that name it (kern:915, kern:916, AC-PF-40, AC-PF-79, AC-AT-66, pipe:1028) are unrunnable.

**What it owns, and what it deliberately does not.**

| The Overlay owns | The Overlay does not own (and must not absorb) |
|---|---|
| The shared **build gate** and **dev source root** that make "absent from release" a file-list fact (DO-2) | Any simulation state: no block, hook, mutator, command, read view or RNG stream (DO-1, DO-9) |
| The one `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription and the model/view split (DO-3, DO-4) | The kernel's diagnostics accessor shape, timer and counters (the kernel's; requested in DO-33) |
| The **channel registration contract** and the naming and severity convention that closes the three sibling "freeze the `diag.*` / `PC_*` / `IA_*` names" asks (DO-12, DO-13) | Any sibling's diagnostic names, counters or panel content (owners register them; the Overlay restates none as a fact) |
| The seven MVP pages as data tables and the generic `P-CHANNELS` page (DO-15) | A per-system panel design for Horror, Body, Lighting, Emitter, Layout, Save/Load, Interaction, Controller, Web Map, Audio, Assembly or Boarding |
| The **discriminator** `desync_classifier` (DO-21) | The Filter's leak math, amplitudes, tick phases or rendering (the Overlay calls `true_value` and `perceived` and displays PF-41's counters) |
| The toggle path, the four modes and the observer-visibility rules (DO-6 to DO-8) | The dev console, `force_stage` / `force_level`, Suit forcing, runtime-knob changes (owners' debug APIs behind the same gate; the Overlay is display only, DO-1) |
| The text `dump` for retained QA evidence (DO-31) | The diagnostics pipeline, CSV or JSON export, hash algorithm (H64) or the K-1 per-block hash (ADR candidates) |

**Tier and size.** Category Meta, layer Polish by dependency (it reads everything), priority **MVP** (index row 26; TD-FEASIBILITY: "needed from the start"). The concept's scope table lists the true-value overlay under Alpha (con:495); the index, written later, and every sibling GDD treat it as MVP, and they win (a concept-document staleness, recorded in Open Questions, not a design conflict). Estimated effort S: the GDD is nevertheless a full eight-section document (the producer's "thin technical spec" recommendation for infrastructure systems was declined, idx:143), and stays proportionate by **breadth**: one registered-channel row per sibling, seven pages as data tables, no per-system panel design, no console, no export pipeline.

**MVP scope (one statement for review and `/create-epics`).** *MVP:* the build gate and the CI file-list check (DO-2); the one `DIAGNOSTIC` subscriber (DO-3); the four modes (DO-6); the seven pages plus the generic `P-CHANNELS` page (DO-15); the `desync_classifier` for class (b) refs (false system states, `CLASSES_ENABLED` {b}) on the local seat (DO-21); `dump` and `mark` (DO-31); and channels for the 15 expected siblings (the 13 siblings of AC-DO-69 (Horror, Body, Lighting, Emitter, Layout, Save/Load, Interaction, Controller, Web Map, Audio, Assembly, Boarding, Suit) plus In-World Instruments and HUD & Suit Layer (15), DO-12). *Vertical Slice B:* the seat cycler, the `MIRROR` own-seat view, `HOST_MIRROR_MISMATCH` (DO-34, AC-DO-44c) and the `harm_eligible` checks for class (c) refs (crewmates as horrors). *Later or spike:* the class (d) phantom-salvage channel, the second-window observer (spike S-3, AC-DO-78), dev `ConfigFile` persistence (OQ-DO-10) and the K-1 per-block hash column (P-10).

## Player Fantasy

*Indirect: the player never meets this system, and never will. They meet a promise it lets the studio keep.* (The register is the kernel's, kern:30. Two audiences are kept apart: the **player's** fantasy is written here, as trust; the **operator's** experience, the developer, QA or playtest observer who actually uses the tool, is written in Game Feel.)

**Every lie was meant, and every lie leaks.** The scrubber gauge in derelict A reads a calm OK while frost thickens on the elbow beside it. The player has to choose what to believe, and the game owes them one thing: the choice must be *fair*. The frost must be the ship, the gauge must be the dream, and the dream must show its seam somewhere (a gleam on the needle, a glide a real needle would not make). Pillar 1: the physical world is ground truth, only instruments and perception can be wrong. Pillar 4: no lie is unfalsifiable, and at least one channel of truth always remains.

A defect breaks that promise silently. A gauge that reads wrong because a value went stale, a rendering path skipped the Filter, or a co-op peer drifted out of step looks *exactly* like a hallucination to the player, but it has no cause, no leak and no way to be disproved. To the player that is not horror; it is the game cheating. The kernel says it plainly: "The fantasy has failed if any player-visible physical consequence ever disagrees with the authoritative state. That is a Pillar 1 violation, not a cosmetic bug — and the reason the Debug Overlay exists" (kern:42).

So the player's fantasy here is **trust in the doubt**: the confidence, earned across sorties, that suspicion is always worth acting on, because the world will answer. The Overlay is the instrument that earns it on the player's behalf. It puts the true value beside the perceived one and gives every difference a name (a lie, a returning lie, or a defect), so the studio can prove each lie was issued and leaked, and each consequence agreed with the ship, before any player has to take it on faith.

**Emotion target, across a player's run (what the Overlay protects, not what it adds):** *suspicion* (the snag in the reading) -> *a check that costs something* (walk to the elbow, feel the cold) -> *the world answers* -> *trust in the doubt* (next time the player acts on the snag sooner). This extends the Filter's arc (pf:59: "familiarity -> the snag -> suspicion with a direction -> quiet certainty -> trained eye"); the Overlay adds no emotion of its own to a run, it guarantees that the last two steps are never false.

**Corollary, the operator** *(the one sane seat aboard)*: the Overlay is the only place that sees both the ship and the dream. It is flat, unlit and plain, and it never lies, because it sits above the pass that does (pipe:102; pf:223 row 9). Its emotion arc is a report ("the gauge lied and I could not tell") -> reproduction -> classification in one glance (a named class, not a judgement call) -> certainty: a lie with a ledger entry and a leak is design, anything else is a ticket (written out in Game Feel).

**Rejected, by name, so nobody re-proposes it:** a "true sight" toggle, streamer mode, assist option or launch flag for curious players. An honest overlay over a lying HUD makes Pillar 4 switchable off (R-7, ab:886), and it fails six already-written release-export file-list acceptance criteria (kern:916, pipe:1028, AC-PF-79, player-controller-input.md:1071, AC-AT-66, AC-SN-85). Project Zomboid's `-debug` flag is an inspiration for the *principle* that debug is a separate mode with its own tools, never for the mechanism: a launch flag on a shipped binary ships the oracle.

**Anchor moment 1: the false-fixed scrubber (what the Overlay certifies)**

MVP slice, derelict A, sanity class (b) only (`CLASSES_ENABLED` {b}). A scrubber node in compartment 2 has failed: Graph `hp` 2400, below `HP_FAIL` 3000 (locked, Graph: `hp` 0..10000, `HP_OK` 7000 / `HP_FAIL` 3000). The player is at sanity stage 1 and Sanity issues a `FALSE_OK` lie on that node; the gauge reads healthy while the frost and the falling temperature, which are honest (Pillar 1), say otherwise. In a dev build, `P-TRUTH` for that `GRAPH_NODE` ref with viewer PlayerId 1 shows the true value, the perceived `displayed_value`, `lie_id`, `lie_kind`, `leak_modes`, the ledger entry and the class. The same row distinguishes three cases, which is the whole mandate in one table (class names are DO-21's; the numeric worked example is in Formulas):

| Variant | What differs | Class / severity | What it means for the player's fantasy |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lie issued, the leak (GLEAM) present | `ledger_active` (DO-21), `lie_id` non-zero, `leak_modes & GLEAM` set | `DELIBERATE` / `NOTE`, tagged `LIE` | A fair lie. The promise holds. |
| Not `ledger_active` (DO-21), `lie_id` 0, yet the gauge still reads OK | Nobody issued a lie; the value is simply wrong | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` / `DEFECT` | The game cheated (stale value, Filter bypass, desync). A ticket. |
| Lie issued, the GLEAM bit clear | The lie has no seam | `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` / `DEFECT` | An unfalsifiable lie, a Pillar 4 violation. A ticket. |

**Anchor moment 2: the playtest observer (the moment the Overlay is *used*)**

The MVP kill/continue playtest (X-BS-11, AC-BS-122). The tester boards derelict B and a creak runs through the hull. The observer, beside the tester, has the dev build in `OBSERVER_STRIP` (one line: `T`, kernel state, Attention `phase`, the seat's sanity `stage`, hash status) and sees `phase TELL`. The tester sees only the ship. The observer notes the tick and whether the tester reacted before the breach, and at the end of the sortie presses `dump`, which writes a text snapshot that goes with a screenshot into `production/qa/evidence/`. This anchor is already a sibling acceptance criterion; it shows Pillars 2 and 3 being *measured* without a meter ever reaching a player; and it makes the oracle risk concrete: if the tester's screen, recording or stream ever shows `FULL` (or the strip, which names the stage and phase), the session's data is void.

*Rejected anchors:* a second-window observer view (multi-window `Window` behaviour in 4.7 is `[UNV, HIGH]`; a spike only); an operator forcing a stage from the Overlay (`force_*` belongs to the owners' debug APIs, the Overlay is display only); any moment where a player sees the Overlay.

**The design test**

> **If a lie is only distinguishable from a defect with the Overlay open, the thing missing is a leak in the world, not a feature in the Overlay: fix the lie (PF-15), never extend the tool toward the player.**

*Corollary (the Pillar 1 test, "a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe"):* if the Overlay is ever the only place a physical consequence can be read, the world is hiding a stat; fix the world.

*Fantasy failure condition (mirrors kern:42):* the fantasy has failed if a playtest report of "the gauge lied and I could not tell" cannot be classified on `P-TRUTH` as `DELIBERATE` or a named `DEFECT_*` within one reproduction, or if any build a player can obtain contains the Overlay.

**How the fantasy deepens, not breaks, under hallucination**

**The Overlay is immune by construction.** It sits at pipeline layer 4 above the Filter pass, and the frame is fed nothing (pipe:102: "no — it reads the true channel"; pf:223 row 9). It *does* read the perceived channel (only through the Filter's `perceived(ref, viewer)` pull; the Overlay does not read the `sanity.perception` block directly, which is read only by the Filter and the replication adapter), but only as data in the perceived column; it is never itself a filtered surface, and its colours never follow the lie (DO-27: true value Paper White, perceived Hull Grey, never violet or flesh even when the world lie leaks violet). There is no "the tool goes mad at stage 3" beat: a corruptible truth tool would destroy the one thing it exists for.

**What deepens is the player's Pillar 4 fantasy, which the Overlay certifies.** The harder the dream pushes, the more lies are in flight and the more the guarantee matters. The page does not get scarier; the guarantee gets stronger: at stage 3 with several lying refs the operator still answers "design or defect?" per row in one glance.

| Pillar 4 class | MVP status | What the Overlay certifies (owner surfaces only) | The defect it catches |
|---|---|---|---|
| (b) False system states | **Enabled** (`CLASSES_ENABLED` {b}) | Per ref: true vs perceived, ledger entry, leak modes, class (DO-21) | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, `DEFECT_NO_LEAK`, `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE`, `DEFECT_BYPASS` |
| (a) Phantom entities | Not enabled at MVP | Horror's own channel: true positions, state, locked point (horror-entities.md:378), registered under DO-12 and shown on `P-CHANNELS` | A hidden real entity with no footprint (Pillar 4: the hallucination hides the body, never the trail), via the owner's counters |
| (c) Crewmates as horrors | Vertical Slice B | `sanity_view(player_id)` `harm_eligible` per seat (contract only, DO-34) | Harm while the attacker was not eligible (an owner AC) |
| (d) Phantom salvage | Later | The owner's channel when designed | None designed here |

**Under the playtest, the tester's dread must stay intact (Pillar 2).** The observer modes exist to keep the truth *off* the tester's screen (`OBSERVER_STRIP`, `HIDDEN`, DO-6, DO-7). A tester who glimpses `FULL` has been handed the oracle Sanity warns about ("a deliberate Pillar 4 oracle risk", sn:174), and that session's data is void.

**Reference techniques: adopt and reject**

References are cited for technique, not as verified facts about those titles' current builds; none is load-bearing.

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| Minecraft's F3 debug screen | Text-first pages on one hotkey, fixed layout, no gameplay effect, readable at a glance | Release reachability: it ships to every player and became player meta (coordinates, biome hunting). Here that would be the oracle. |
| Factorio's debug options | Per-system world overlays toggled independently (outlines, cones, rects, true horror positions), each owned by the owning system's registered channel | Player-accessible toggles; per-system pages designed inside the tool (scope absorption) |
| Source-engine `net_graph` style readouts | The one-line compact strip for watching without reading | Showing it to the person being measured |
| Project Zomboid `-debug` | The principle that debug is a separate mode with its own tools | A launch flag on a shipped binary: it ships the oracle and anyone can type it |
| Celeste Assist Mode and accessibility toggles in general | Nothing for this system (Accessibility Settings owns the three Filter scalars, registry `filter_accessibility_scalars`) | The analogy itself: making a challenge switchable for a player is exactly what R-7 forbids when the challenge is Pillar 4 |
| Eternal Darkness / Amnesia style sanity meters | Nothing | Any sanity, attention or lie-count readout outside the Overlay root (PF-38, AC-AT-66, AC-SN-146) |
| Pandorum / Beyond the Aquila Rift (the "what was real" reveal) | Nothing for the Overlay; the reveal belongs to the world (the room gets cold, the crate was garbage all along) | Using the Overlay's true-beside-perceived layout as the template for any in-game "clarity" item, scanner mode or ending reveal (Pillar 1, Pillar 4, anti-pillar: no authored story) |

**Pillar and anti-pillar guard rails**

These are stated here as fantasy-level guard rails and made testable in Core Rules (DO-30, the Never list) and Acceptance Criteria.

| Guard rail | Source |
|---|---|
| **Never a player surface.** No setting, menu entry, "developer mode" toggle, rebinding entry, screen-reader node, tutorial line, achievement or launch flag in any build a player can obtain. | R-7; P-3, P-4, P-7 (Dependencies) |
| **Never a write surface.** No submit, mutator, `force_*` button, spawn, teleport, knob slider or rewind. Display only. | kern:117, kern:203; anti-pillar: not a freeform builder |
| **Never a meter outside its root.** No Attention level, sanity stage, lie count or verdict appears anywhere but the Overlay root. | Pillar 3 ("never as a hidden meter"), PF-38, AC-AT-66 |
| **Never corruptible.** Above the Filter pass; true value never tinted by the lie; no violet, no flesh; Alarm Red only as a mark beside a `DEFECT`; `NOT_USED` never red; `UNAVAILABLE` never shown as `HONEST`. | Pillar 1 (the sane palette is inviolable), pf:223 row 9, AC-PF-38 |
| **Never on the tester's screen in `FULL`.** Playtest builds launch `HIDDEN`; the observer uses `OBSERVER_STRIP`. | X-BS-11, AC-BS-122 |
| **Never audible.** No sonification, no beep on `DEFECT`; audio counters are displayed, never played. | Pillar 2 (audio tells belong to the world) |
| **Never part of the world.** Not a block, not in a snapshot, not in `state_hash`; Overlay on vs off yields an identical hash sequence. | AC-AT-56 shape |
| **Adds no verb and no knowledge.** Not a Pillar 5 "knowledge persists" channel, not a combat or PvP aid, not lore. What a player learns, they learn from the world. | Pillar 5; anti-pillars: not a combat game, no intentional PvP, no authored story |

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

**Conventions.** Rules are `DO-n` (named); edge cases are `E-DO-nn`, acceptance criteria `AC-DO-nn`, tuning knobs `DIAG_*` / `OVERLAY_*`, all in the later sections. Tags follow the sibling GDDs: `[REF]` = in `docs/engine-reference/godot/`; `[UNV]` = unverified against the engine (the reference has **no** scripting, core, export or debugging file, so export presets and filters, feature tags, `OS.has_feature`, `OS.is_debug_build`, the `Performance` singleton, `Viewport.get_render_info`, `EngineDebugger` and multi-window `Window` are all `[UNV]`, knowledge-gap risk HIGH for 4.5 to 4.7); `[PLACEHOLDER]` = invented here, carrying a range and a proposed registry name, final nowhere. "-> ADR candidate" marks an implementation choice this GDD does not make. Every name from another GDD is used verbatim; a sibling's *provisional* name is cited with its source line and the word "provisional" and is never restated as a fact. Each rule gives the *Rule*, the *Why* and the acceptance-criteria group that proves it. Where a specialist proposal and a ruling differed, a one-line "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" note says which was taken.

**Reading order.** Authority and gate: DO-1 (display only), DO-2 (build gate and source root), DO-3 (one subscriber), DO-9 (no sim state). Structure and layer: DO-4 (model/view), DO-5 (layer 4). Modes, input and viewer: DO-6 to DO-8, DO-10. Kernel states and boundaries: DO-11, DO-32 to DO-34. The channel and page contract: DO-12 to DO-15, DO-26. Reads, hashes, timings and counters: DO-16 to DO-19. The true/perceived pair and the discriminator: DO-20 to DO-23. The Sanity and Attention pages: DO-24, DO-25. Presentation, inertness, the Never list and evidence: DO-27 to DO-31. The channel contract, classifier and seven page tables follow the rules.

**DO-1. Display-only `DIAGNOSTIC` reader.** *Rule:* The Overlay is a kernel subscriber of class `DIAGNOSTIC`. It reads views and the kernel's diagnostics surface and writes nothing. It submits no command, calls no mutator, calls no owner's debug write function, and owns no block, no hook, no read view and no RNG stream. `force_stage`, `force_level`, Suit forcing and runtime-knob changes (honoured at P6, kern:734) belong to the owners' debug APIs, reached from the dev console (owed by the lead-programmer; kern:734, player-controller-input.md:742, suit-and-equipment.md:1317) behind the DO-2 gate; the Overlay only *displays* their result (a `FORCED` tag, DO-24). *Why:* kern:117 ("Subscriber handlers run with writes disabled... `INPUT_SURFACE` is the one class that may submit"); kern:203 row 11 ("Neither gets submit or mutators"); CR-9. A button that forced a stage would make the Overlay a write surface and break CR-9's `WriteScope` rule. *(orchestrator ruling: display only. Routed: Sanity's own row, sn:1352, lists `force_stage` / `force_level` beside the Overlay; a one-clause clarification is requested, "reached from the dev console, displayed by the Overlay". The tension between a debug-only `force_*` and CR-9 is the kernel owner's question and exists regardless of this GDD.)* *AC group:* inertness, gating.

**DO-2. One gate, one dev source root.** *Rule:* All Overlay code, scenes, the world debug-draw node and the Overlay's dev actions live under **one dev source root**, placeholder `src/debug/` with the Overlay at `src/debug/overlay/` `[PLACEHOLDER]`, final path owned by architecture, proposed registry name `overlay_source_root`. Non-dev export presets exclude that root by an export resource filter `[UNV, HIGH]`. The composition root registers the `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription entry point and the dev-only actions **only** when a dev feature tag (placeholder `dev`, `[UNV]`) is present. A CI step lists the release export's files and fails the build on any path under the root. "Zero cost in Release" therefore means *nothing loaded*, not a runtime branch. The gate, the root and the CI step are **shared** by every dev seam guarded elsewhere (`force_*`, `--filter-test-freeze-dither`, `IA_PHANTOM_PROBE`, the dev input-event logger, the tree-walk script and the flash-scan tool, the dev console, the Input Adapter's `PC_*` presentation-diagnostics surface and its `debug_overlay_*` action registration); proposed registry name `overlay_build_gate`, and one versioned list of known dev artefacts (proposed `dev_artifact_list`) that every file-list check reuses. *Why:* six already-written acceptance criteria inspect the release export **file list**, not a runtime branch: kern:916, pipe:1028, AC-PF-79 (pf:1846), player-controller-input.md:1071, AC-AT-66 (at:1572) and AC-SN-85. A runtime `if OS.is_debug_build()` fails all six, and a flag can be flipped and still ship the oracle (Pillar 4, R-7). The same root makes the lints that allow-list "the Debug Overlay" implementable (AC-AT-66, AC-PF-51, AC-SN-74, AC-SN-146). *(orchestrator ruling: file-level exclusion plus gated registration; a runtime flag alone and a separate-project conditional compile were rejected, GDScript having no `#if`.)* *Engine gap:* export filters and feature tags are `[UNV, HIGH]`; one spike story (release-export an empty scene plus one file under the root, list the files, record `OS.has_feature` results in editor, dev export and release export). -> ADR candidate (debug-gate / export-filter). *AC group:* absence and gating.

**DO-3. Exactly one subscriber.** *Rule:* There is one `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription for the whole Overlay, with the DO-12 channel registry behind it, and therefore one entry point to strip. The handler only **copies** the P5 notification batch `(block_id, tick, change_keys)` (CR-10) and marks the model dirty; it does no formatting, no classification and no I/O. TellAudit (AT-15) stays Attention's own `DIAGNOSTIC` fixture; the Overlay only displays its result (DO-25). *Why:* one entry point to exclude from release (DO-2); handlers must be cheap (kern:114); a throwing subscriber is isolated by the kernel, which logs the subscriber id and stays `RUNNING` with `T` advanced (kern:867). *(orchestrator ruling: one subscriber, not one per panel or per system.)* *AC group:* gating, panel-fault isolation.

**DO-4. Model/view split, pure model.** *Rule:* A pure `OverlayModel` (no scene tree) is built from `(diagnostics snapshot, read views, registered channels)`; an `OverlayView` Control tree pulls from the model at render rate. The classifier and every formula are model functions, unit-testable headless under `tests/unit/debug_overlay/`. The model holds **copies**, never a live reference to a block (GDScript has no const references, so a read-only view over a block is a copy or a frozen wrapper; kernel OQ 8). This GDD describes behaviour (what is pure, what is cached, what is copied), not class code. -> ADR candidate (node and class layout, copy strategy). *Why:* testability (coding standards: dependency injection over singletons); writes-disabled is a kernel-enforced property, not the Overlay's; the Overlay must not hold a reference that could be mutated through. *AC group:* classifier, pages.

**DO-5. Layer 4, the truth channel.** *Rule:* The view is a root-viewport `CanvasLayer` at pipeline layer 4, integer-scaled at the HUD scale (`H_default = max(k, 3)`, pipe:224 (PR-12), pipe:373) plus the placeholder `OVERLAY_SCALE_OFFSET`, above the HUD (layer 2) and chart table and menus (layer 3). It receives **no** Filter pass, no lighting, no camera jolt and no grade (pipe:102; pf:223 row 9: the pass sits below every `CanvasLayer`, the frame is fed nothing). It carries no Filter-fed value except the `perceived` column, which is labelled as perceived. Non-interactive panels use `MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE`; the Overlay never takes gameplay focus (4.6 dual focus `[REF modules/ui.md]`; the 4.5 recursive mouse-filter behaviour is also in the reference). *Why:* the truth channel must not itself be corruptible (Pillar 1); the Overlay is one of the surfaces the Filter pass never reaches (pf:223 row 9). *AC group:* palette, gating, focus.

**DO-6. Four modes.** (registry: `overlay_modes`) *Rule:* `OFF` (registered, handler returns at once, nothing collected), `FULL` (all pages), `OBSERVER_STRIP` (one line, DO-7) and `HIDDEN` (subscribed and collecting, drawing nothing); transitions in States and Transitions. The default on a dev launch is `OFF`; a tester-session launch passes a launch option (placeholder `--overlay=hidden`) read by the composition root. A mode change takes effect within one rendered frame and never alters `T`. *Why:* X-BS-11, AC-BS-122 and AC-SN-88 need an observer read that is never shown to the tester. *(orchestrator ruling: four modes; a playtest build is a dev build launched `HIDDEN`; there is no third build flavour in MVP.)* *AC group:* modes and observer.

**DO-7. Observer strip and the oracle rule.** *Rule:* `OBSERVER_STRIP` is one text line: `T`, kernel state, Attention `phase`, the viewer's sanity `stage` and hash status (`OK` / `n/a` / `DRIFT` plus the age of `hash_T`). It shows no ledger field, no true/perceived pair and no ref; the hash status is the age of `hash_T` with the algorithm tag as DO-17 defines it (the kernel's `hash_algo` field, granted 2026-10-27; `H64 (pending ADR)` only if it is absent). `FULL` and the strip are **tester-hostile**: a tester's screen, recording or stream must never carry either (the strip names the stage and the phase). `HIDDEN` is only safe because leaving it needs the reveal chord (DO-8). *Why:* sn:174 calls the ledger "a deliberate Pillar 4 oracle risk"; the Overlay is the only reader outside `sanity/` (AC-SN-74). The risk is a process risk the code can only mitigate, not remove, and the GDD says so plainly. *AC group:* modes and observer (the strip contains no ref and no ledger field; `HIDDEN` draws no Overlay node).

**DO-8. Toggle input path and reveal chord.** *Rule:* The Controller's Input Adapter registers the Overlay's dev-only actions only under the DO-2 tag and forwards them to the Overlay by a host-local call: never a command, never a binding-table entry visible to Settings, never in the rebinding or accessibility enumerations. Placeholder names (registry: `debug_overlay_actions`): `debug_overlay_toggle`, `debug_overlay_page_next`, `debug_overlay_page_prev`, `debug_overlay_mode`, `debug_overlay_dump`, and two added here, `debug_overlay_reveal` and `debug_overlay_mark`, and three for selection: `debug_overlay_cursor_next`, `debug_overlay_cursor_prev` (move a row cursor over the current page's selectable rows: refs and the viewer row on `P-TRUTH`, channels and draw toggles on `P-CHANNELS`) and `debug_overlay_activate` (pin or unpin the ref under the cursor, cycle the viewer, select the channel, flip the draw toggle); all three act only in `FULL`. Placeholder defaults: toggle F3 (verify no editor conflict `[UNV]`), page next and previous F4 and Shift+F4, mode cycle F5, dump F6, mark F7, cursor next and previous F8 and Shift+F8, activate F9, reveal chord Ctrl+Shift+F3; gamepad: the reveal chord is both stick clicks; other gamepad bindings `[PLACEHOLDER]`. The row cursor is dev-session state (DO-9), wraps at the ends of the page's selectable rows, and `activate` on a row with no action is ignored. In `HIDDEN` only the reveal chord acts, and it reveals to `OBSERVER_STRIP`, not `FULL`, so showing a lie takes a second deliberate action. The actions are `StringName` action names and use `InputEvent.DEVICE_ID_*`, not `device == 0` `[REF breaking-changes.md 4.7]`. *Why:* the Controller lint "no `Input` reads outside the Adapter" (player-controller-input.md:58 PC-1, :1026); a tester brushing the plain toggle must not reveal the oracle. *(orchestrator ruling: the Adapter-forwarded dev action set, plus the reveal chord, the mark action and the three selection actions as refinements; the Controller-owner amendment request grows by five action names and joins the open lint collision at player-controller-input.md:1097.)* *AC group:* input path, enumeration negatives.

**DO-9. Dev-session state only.** *Rule:* Mode, page, row cursor, selected viewer, selected ref, pinned refs, observer marks and every history window are dev-session state: never a block, never in a snapshot, never in `state_hash`, never saved across launches in MVP (a dev `ConfigFile` under `user://` is deferred, Open Questions). *Why:* a persisted Overlay block would enter `state_hash` and break the G4 comparison with the Overlay on versus off (AC-AT-56 shape). *AC group:* inertness.

**DO-10. One viewer.** *Rule:* The Overlay has one selectable viewer `PlayerId`, defaulting to the local seat (MVP: PlayerId 1) and always shown in the header; the operator cycles it with `activate` on the viewer row of `P-TRUTH`, reached with `cursor_next` / `cursor_prev` (DO-8), and each cycle resets the per-`(ref, viewer)` counters (AC-DO-77). Addressing is by `PlayerId`, never `peer_id` (pf:271, PF-10). Because the Filter answers for the **local seat only** and returns the honest record for a remote `PlayerId` while counting `remote_queries` (PF-10), the Overlay **never calls `perceived` for a remote seat**: a viewer other than the local seat classifies `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `REMOTE_VIEWER`, and `P-TRUTH` shows "perceived: not queried". This keeps `remote_queries` a pure leakage indicator and prevents a seat-B lie from classifying `HONEST` against its own honest-record answer. Vertical Slice B adds a seat cycler (contract only, DO-34). *(orchestrator ruling: one selectable viewer, refined by the finding that the Filter answers for the local seat only, PF-10.)* *AC group:* classifier, viewer switch.

**DO-11. Behaviour in every kernel state.** *Rule:* The Overlay is drawable in all six kernel states (table in States and Transitions). In a dev build `FAULTED` halts the kernel with diagnostics (kern:151); the render loop and the Overlay keep running and show the FAULT record `(T, type, issuer, host_order, block_id, assertion)` while the kernel is not ticking (chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md:377 accepts "Dev builds halt with the Debug Overlay (not ours)" as written). `BOOTING` shows boot progress and, on failure, the unknown `hook_id` or the cycle path (kern:606). `HIDDEN` stays hidden even on `FAULTED`: a tester must not see it either. *Why:* the Overlay is the tool one reaches for when the kernel is least healthy. *AC group:* kernel states.

**DO-12. Channel registration contract.** *Rule:* Every system that wants a diagnostics presence registers a **channel** at composition-root time under the DO-2 tag. A channel declares the fields in the contract table below (proposed registry name `diag_panel_contract`). The Overlay renders whatever is registered on the generic page `P-CHANNELS` and restates no owner's name as a fact. *Why:* it closes the three open asks "the Debug Overlay GDD should freeze the names" (pipe:1055, player-controller-input.md:1095, interaction-system.md:731) as **owners freeze their own names, using this convention**, and honours Body's "use the exact diagnostic names above" (ship-body-and-interior-physics.md:898) by construction. Freezing six systems' tables here would repeat the 2026-10-26 placeholder-becomes-fact failure and go stale on the first sibling edit. Transport and format of any diagnostics pipeline stay the AT-32 ADR candidate. *(orchestrator ruling: panel contract and naming convention here; names stay with their owners.)* *AC group:* channel contract.

**DO-13. Naming convention and severity vocabulary.** *Rule:* Channel and row names are snake_case `<system>_<noun>` or the owner's existing prefix (`PC_*`, `IA_*`, `BDY_*`, `graph_*`, `diag.*`, all provisional where their owner says so). Units are in the declaration, never in the name; a counter name does not end in `_ms` or `_us` unless the owner's registry name already does. The severity vocabulary is fixed at four levels: `OK`, `NOTE`, `WARN`, `DEFECT` (registry: `diag_severity_vocabulary`). A row declares its severity as a rule over its own value (for example "`> 0` is `WARN`"); the Overlay evaluates the rule and never invents one for an owner. A `DEFECT` rule must cite the owner's rule it enforces. *Why:* this resolves the mismatch between the brief's `OK/WARN/DEFECT` and `OK/NOTE/DEFECT`. Severity is always a text tag and colour is secondary (DO-27). *AC group:* channel contract.

**DO-14. Per-panel fault isolation.** *Rule:* Each channel `pull()` and each page render is wrapped so an error in one does not stop the others. The failing channel shows one `DEFECT` row naming its `channel_id` and the error class, and after `DIAG_PANEL_ERR_LATCH_N` consecutive failures the channel is suspended until the next mode change. A channel not registered at first draw shows `PENDING`; a channel with no update for `DIAG_CHANNEL_STALE_S` shows `STALE` (`NOTE`). A diagnostics tool must not take the build down. *Why:* the kernel already isolates a throwing subscriber (kern:867); this is the Overlay's own inner layer of the same guarantee. *AC group:* panel fault.

**DO-15. Page set.** *Rule:* Seven MVP pages, each specified as a data table of (row, source, unit, cadence, severity rule), below: `P-KERNEL`, `P-HOOKS`, `P-STATE`, `P-TRUTH`, `P-SANITY`, `P-ATTENTION`, `P-FRAME`. Every other system appears only as a registered channel on the generic page `P-CHANNELS` (a channel list plus the selected channel's rows and its world debug-draw toggles); that is not an eighth designed panel. Page order is fixed and `page_next` / `page_prev` cycle `P-KERNEL` to `P-FRAME` and then `P-CHANNELS`. No page designs a layout; layout is the view's affair. *Why:* the seven pages cover the six routed items (hash, dirty keys, hook timings, the Graph `true_value`, Attention level, sanity per player) plus the PF-41 contract and the Pipeline surface while keeping a system sized S. *AC group:* pages.

**DO-16. Reads only published names.** *Rule:* The Overlay reads a system only through that system's published read view or diagnostics channel: Graph `true_value(kind, id)`, `block_hash()`, `summary()`, `failures(filter)`, `compartment(cid)`, `power_use(section_id)` (lsg:199); Suit `true_value(key, player_id)` (SE-29); Sanity `sanity_view(player_id)`, `sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger` (SN-28, **sn:335-337**, cited as the paragraph, not the bare sn:336); Attention `attention.state`, `attention.view`; Horror `horror.view`; the Filter's `perceived` and the PF-41 counters. It never reaches into a block it does not own a view of. The ledger is `HOST_ONLY` (provisional on a mirror, DO-34): on a `MIRROR` the ledger is unreadable and rows needing it classify `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `NO_LEDGER`. *Why:* the ledger lint (AC-SN-74) names the Overlay as the one non-Sanity reader; the Overlay is the sole sanctioned `true_value` caller beside the Filter (AC-PF-51). *AC group:* gating (lint), cross-system.

**DO-17. Hash display: locked versus provisional.** *Rule:* The Overlay shows the kernel's `state_hash` exactly as last published, with `hash_T` (the tick it was computed at), its age `T - hash_T` and the algorithm tag. **`hash_algo` is not a kernel-published name** (the kernel names only `H64`, kern:531, "xxHash64 or FNV-1a 64, pending ADR", D.12; a search of the kernel GDD finds no `hash_algo`): it was a **field this GDD requested** in the P-9 accessor (DO-33a), granted by the kernel 2026-10-27; if it is absent the algorithm cell reads `H64 (pending ADR)`. With the field present, a hash from another `hash_algo` is never compared. The per-block column is the owner-published `block_hash()` where one exists (Graph LS-19 and LS-21, Emitter `block_hash()`, Layout `layout_hash`) and `n/a` otherwise; if the kernel adopts K-1 (a per-block cached hash, **routed, not adopted**, lsg:1155; sl:1290) the column switches to the kernel's per-block hash (P-10). The Overlay **never hashes anything itself**, on a timer or on demand. The cadence is the kernel's (DO-33). *Why:* K-1 is "routed, not adopted; D.12 stands until the kernel owner amends" (lsg:1155), and OQ-17 (sl:1316) warns that a GDScript 64-bit hash over 100 to 300 KiB may exceed 1.5 ms, so a hash per tick would put 90 ms of hashing into every real second. *Split:* the `state_hash` formula (kern D.12) is locked; per-block hashing and the cadence are provisional. *(orchestrator ruling: the kernel computes, the Overlay displays.)* *AC group:* pages (hash), unblocked sibling ACs (kern:915).

**DO-18. Dirty-key accounting with peak-hold.** *Rule:* In the P5 batch the handler records, per `(block_id, tick)`, the key count (or an `ALL` collapse, kern D.9). Each refresh the model folds the ticks since the last refresh into `last`, `peak` and `collapse_count` per block. `dirty_load_pm` (Formulas) is shown against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, **never altered**. An `ALL` collapse is `WARN`, not `DEFECT`: it is legal kernel behaviour, and a restore legitimately produces one `ALL` then silence. The Graph's measured worst cases (about 38 keys MVP, about 135 VS-A, lsg:201 LS-20) are the reference points. Every `DEFECT`-capable counter and timing is **peak-held** across the folded ticks, never sampled: numerals refresh at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` (10) but the kernel emits at `TICK_RATE` 60 and the Graph runs on every sixth tick, so a one-tick spike would otherwise fall between refreshes. *Why:* a cadence-blind display would hide the very events it exists to catch. *AC group:* pages (dirty keys, peak-hold).

**DO-19. Hook timings: registry names, indicative on the dev template.** *Rule:* `P-HOOKS` prints the kernel's resolved hook order (kern:607: "printed by the Debug Overlay"; identical every boot) and each hook's `t_hook` as the kernel publishes it, plus the kernel's `step_over_budget` flag, **displayed, not recomputed** (kern D.6; `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is "a threshold, not a cap"). It adds one derived quantity: `graph_hook_sum_run = t(graph.propagate.power) + t(graph.propagate.atmo) + t(graph.propagate.failure)` per Graph run, with a rolling P99 and a maximum over `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` runs (`hook_window_p99`, Formulas) compared with `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` (registry `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`: 0.5 P99 / 1.0 max per run (MVP); 1.0 P99 per tick (VS-A)). **The figure is indicative:** the budget is defined on the **Release export template** where the Overlay is absent, so the Overlay's number is a dev-template number; the gate is the Graph's S1 spike and perf gate. A breach of the indicative figure is `WARN`, never `DEFECT`. The Overlay reads no wall clock of its own for these (the kernel's diagnostics timer is the one allow-listed reader, H9, kern:781). *Why:* the budget is a P99 of a *sum* of three hooks per run, while the kernel exposes per-hook `t_hook` only; the reflexion log (2026-10-26, two-bound formula) says to register the formula, not only its constants. The registry name wins over the Graph's row label for the same quantity (lsg:724): the Overlay never surfaces a second, differently named constant. *AC group:* pages (hooks), performance.

**DO-20. Ref picker and the true/perceived pair.** *Rule:* `P-TRUTH` lists the refs to inspect: first every ref for which `ledger_active` holds (DO-21), then the refs the Filter has been asked about this session, then pinned refs (capped at `DIAG_TRUTH_REFS_MAX`). The operator moves the row cursor over this list with `cursor_next` / `cursor_prev`; the ref under the cursor is the selected ref, and `activate` pins or unpins it (DO-8); a pin is dev-session state (DO-9). For the selected ref, in **one rendered frame**, the model reads, in this order and before any other callback: `tv` from the owner (Graph `true_value(kind, id)` for `GRAPH_NODE` and `GRAPH_COMPARTMENT`; Suit `true_value(key, player_id)` for `SUIT_KEY`; the Graph node state for `STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW` and `MACHINE_FRAME` through the Filter's ref-to-subject mapping, *provisional* per PF-9; `SCANNER` has no published true source and is `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `NO_TRUE_SOURCE` until the Filter publishes one), then `p = perceived(ref, viewer)` (a hit on the Filter's per-`(ref, viewer, frame)` memo, pf:247, allocation-free on the honest path), then the matching ledger entry and the Filter counters. It shows side by side: the true value, the perceived `displayed_value` and `displayed_pm`, `lie_id`, `lie_kind`, `leak_modes` (GLEAM / GLIDE / SOUND), `leak_amplitude_pm`, `motion_mode`, the ledger `start_run` and `end_run`, `disc`, `tol`, the class and its severity. The true value is never tinted by the lie. *Why:* PF-41; the one-frame read stops `tv` and `p` straddling a tick, which would flag a false `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` on a tick boundary. *(orchestrator ruling: the qa-lead's "same frame" gap is closed here: one refresh reads both before any other callback, using the Filter's memo.)* *AC group:* pages (truth), classifier, inertness (a second `perceived` call hits the memo).

**DO-21. The discriminator `desync_classifier`.** *Rule:* The model classifies each listed `(ref, viewer)` at the refresh cadence by a pure function (formula in Formulas; proposed registry name `desync_classifier`); class table below. Evaluation order is fixed and the **first match wins**; `UNAVAILABLE` is first and `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` is the explicit catch-all, so no input falls through. **Honest means exact equality** `p.displayed_value == tv` (pf:247: the honest path is exact by rule). **`ledger_active(ref, viewer)`** (the Overlay's only reading of the Sanity ledger; it replaces every earlier "has a ledger entry"): true iff `sanity.ledger.lies[]` holds an entry for `viewer` whose `subject_ref` is the ref's subject (the Filter's PF-9 ref-to-subject map, *provisional*) and whose `state` is **`ACTIVE`**. A Sanity row in `PENDING` (onset delay `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` 10 to 25 runs, 2 to 5 s, not yet in `sanity.perception`) and the ended states `EXPIRED`, `DEBUNKED` and `INVALIDATED` are **not** active. With two `ACTIVE` entries on one subject the Overlay matches the lowest `(start_run, slot)`; the Filter draws the lowest `(start_run, lie_id)` (PF-46, pf:467), so on a `start_run` tie the two may differ: the row shows `lie_collisions` and a "tie: pairing unverified" note until OQ-DO-1 settles `lie_id` provenance. The join is by `(viewer, subject_ref)`, **never by `lie_id`**: the ledger row has a `slot` and no `lie_id` (sn:170, sn:314; the `sanity.perception` whitelist of AC-SN-136), while the Filter's `lie_id` is a Filter-side value (OQ-DO-1 asks the two owners to settle its provenance, pf:979 versus sn:314). The tolerance `tol(kind)` is used only for the `RETURNING` and `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` tests and is **the owner's own dirty-key quantum for that field**, cited as such and never as a display quantum: Graph LS-20 (lsg:201): `T` 1000 mC, `P` 10 dkPa, `p_o2` 5 dkPa, store 20 permille, intensity 100 permille, `hp` 250 bp; Suit vitals buckets (suit-and-equipment.md:270-277 (SE-38)): reserve and cell 5 percent of maximum, `integrity_bp` 250 bp; binary-frame refs (`STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`, and any ref carrying a `SPRITE_SWAP` lie, including a `FALSE_OK` or `FALSE_FAIL` drawn on a housing appearance frame, pf:82, PF-19) compare as 0 or 1 with `tol` 0. `FALSE_OK` and `FALSE_FAIL` on a needle dial or digital readout are continuous zone-targeting lies (Filter PF-13, D.3 `target_pm`; STALE readout, PF-18): they use the owner's quantum, `tol_eff` and the `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` reach age exactly like `GLIDE`. `LEAK_EPS_DEG` 0.25 applies **only** to the optional drawn-versus-perceived probe (DO-22), which compares in needle-angle space. No tolerance value is invented. Fields for which the owner publishes no quantum (`o2_pm`, `n_o2`, `n_inert`) use `tol` 0 until the Graph owner lists one (requested, OQ-DO-3). **Effective tolerance:** a lying `displayed_value` is quantised to one per mille of the instrument range, because `displayed_value` derives from the integer `displayed_pm` (PF-8, pf:670), so a `tol` of 0 would let rounding jitter fail an honest return. The classifier therefore uses `tol_eff(ref) = max(tol(kind), q_display(ref))`, with `q_display = (value_hi - value_lo) / 1000` from the Filter's PF-45 calibration record (published on the Filter's dev channel, registry `filter_counters`, OQ-DO-2 (ii) resolved 2026-10-27; **for a ref whose calibration is unavailable, kinds with `tol` 0 skip the step sub-test of `RETURNING` and are bounded only by `t_return_max`**). `HONEST` stays exact equality. Boundary rule: `disc == tol_eff` is *inside* the tolerance. *Why:* Pillar 1 (an uncaused difference is always a defect) and Pillar 4 (a lie with no leak is a defect). *Reconciliations, each an orchestrator ruling:* **(1) `RETURNING` is a wander-safe closure test bounded by `t_return_max`, never by `LEAK_FADE_S`.** The first-draft ruling bounded `RETURNING` by `LEAK_FADE_S` 1.5 s and `|disc| <= tol`. The Filter's return is a rate-limited follower at `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` that tracks a live true value and takes `t_return = disc_end / (rm x GLIDE_RETURN_RATE)` for a static true value, at most `D_MAX_FRAC_ARC x arc_deg / GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` = 96 / 3 = 32 s at `rm` 1.0 and 96 / (0.6 x 3.0) = 53.3 s at `rm` 0.6 (`rm` is latched per lie, E-PF-43; the Filter's own example, pf D.6, is a 60-deg discrepancy: 20 s at 3.0 deg/s and 33.3 s under reduced motion). `LEAK_FADE_S` bounds only the amplitude fade when `disc_end <= 2 x LEAK_EPS_DEG` (pf:731). Read literally, a `LEAK_FADE_S` bound would flag the first 18 s of an honest return as `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`. The Filter's locked behaviour wins: `RETURNING` holds iff `t_ret <= t_return_max(lie)`, **published by the Filter per lie on its dev channel (registry `filter_counters`)** (it depends on `rm`, so it is never a constant of this GDD), and the discrepancy behaves as a return. Because the Filter's wander term keeps running for `WANDER_BLEND_S` (3 s, cited) into `RETURN` (`w_ret_pm` fades out with the return, pf D.6), a per-step monotone test would false-flag an honest return, so the test has two phases: for `t_ret < WANDER_BLEND_S` the **envelope** `disc_t <= disc_end + abs(tv_t - tv_end) + tol_eff`; for `t_ret >= WANDER_BLEND_S` the **step test** `disc_t <= disc_{t-1} + abs(tv_t - tv_{t-1}) + tol_eff` at refresh cadence (the second term lets a moving true value be tracked: the follower is monotone toward it and cannot overshoot). **Binary-frame refs** (`SPRITE_SWAP`, `STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`) cut back at removal (pf:778), so a non-zero `disc` on one of them in `RETURN` is `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` (the gleam decay is not a value discrepancy). `LEAK_FADE_S` is not restated with any other value. Formula, variables and three worked returns are in Formulas (`desync_classifier`). **(2) Two classes are added** to the first-draft list: `SKEW` and `DEFECT_BYPASS`. `SKEW` is the lie-presence skew between Sanity and the Filter (Sanity rebuilds `sanity.perception` each run, five runs per second, and the Filter reads it on the next frame); it was drafted as `PENDING` and **renamed `SKEW` at the checkpoint** because `PENDING` is already Sanity's ledger state (sn:388-389) and this GDD's channel status. `DEFECT_BYPASS` is implied by the render-probe ask (DO-22). **(3) Precedence**: the tools-programmer's guarded predicates are kept, with defects ranked above notes so that no `NOTE` can shadow a `DEFECT` when two classes overlap, with exactly two by-design exceptions that rank first: `UNAVAILABLE` (an input is missing, so no defect can be asserted) and `SKEW` (bounded by `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES`, so it cannot hide a defect for longer than a few frames). The qa-lead's alternative order, `UNAVAILABLE` > `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` > `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` > `DELIBERATE` > `RETURNING` > `HONEST` > `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, agrees on the defect-first principle and omits only the two added classes. **(4) `lie_id` during `RETURN` is not assumed.** The Filter says the entry is absent in `RETURN` (pf:502: "`RETURN`: entry absent and discrepancy at or above `LEAK_EPS_DEG`") and the Filter has confirmed that `lie_id` is kept through `RETURN` and is 0 once the follower closes (registry `filter_counters`; OQ-DO-2 (v) resolved 2026-10-27). The classifier is independent of the answer: `SKEW` counts `lie_id != 0` only while `motion_mode != RETURN`, and `RETURNING` tests `motion_mode == RETURN` and not `ledger_active` with no `lie_id` test. *AC group:* classifier (one table-driven row per class).

**DO-22. Render-probe: an optional input.** *Rule:* The Overlay asks In-World Instruments (designed (this run); P-2, accepted by IW-38) for a dev-only `drawn_value(ref)` per corruptible node: the displayed value the node actually drew this frame. When present, the class `DEFECT_BYPASS` (drawn value differs from `p.displayed_value` by more than `LEAK_EPS_DEG` in needle-angle space) detects a node that skipped the Filter. When absent, the classifier stops at `perceived` versus `true_value` versus ledger, and the row's `bypass_check` field reads `ABSENT`; it is never shown as clean and the row's class is not changed to `UNAVAILABLE` on that account alone. *(accepted by Instruments IW-38; until the probe is live in a build, `bypass_check` reads `ABSENT`.)* *Why:* a node that bypasses the Filter looks exactly like a defect and is invisible without the probe; and this is the only place `LEAK_EPS_DEG` is used (it is a needle-angle epsilon in degrees, not a quantum for mGU, mC, dkPa, permille or bp). *AC group:* classifier.

**DO-23. Non-reference desync classes.** (non-reference classes; registry: `desync_classes`) *Rule:* `P-STATE` shows three classes that do not belong to a ref. `HASH_DRIFT` comes from Save/Load's SL-12 reproduction compare (after the last `post_restore`, before the first `RUNNING` tick) or a G4 mismatch, and names the first differing `block_id` where its owner publishes `block_hash()`. `PERCEPTION_IN_HASH` means the hash differs with the Filter on versus off over an unchanged sim (AC-AT-56 shape) and is **displayed from a test-harness result**, never computed live by the Overlay. `HOST_MIRROR_MISMATCH` is Vertical Slice B (P-6), defined, deferred and shown as `n/a`. *Why:* the index's high-risk row (idx:281) is real desync, not only lies. *AC group:* pages (state).

**DO-24. Sanity page.** *Rule:* `P-SANITY` shows, per `PlayerId`, the values in the page table below. The Overlay displays `force_*` state and never invokes it. A seat whose stage or level was set by `force_stage` or `force_level` carries a `FORCED` tag so that a forced stage is never mistaken for an earned one. The tag is fed by the per-seat `forced` dev diagnostic flag in `sanity.state` (SN-28; granted by Sanity 2026-10-27, OQ-DO-4 resolved); the Overlay never infers a forced seat from the level. *Why:* SN-28 (sn:335-337); the page makes AC-SN-88 readable and unblocks AC-SN-144 (named blocker "deferred: Debug Overlay"). *AC group:* pages (sanity), unblocked sibling ACs.

**DO-25. Attention page.** *Rule:* `P-ATTENTION` shows the values in the page table below. A TellAudit violation, or a `tell_len` below `TELL_MIN_TICKS` on an endogenous tell, is `DEFECT`. In `CALM` the TellAudit panel is labelled "last audit at T=..." so that a stale result is never read as current. The raw `level_mAU` and `tell_remaining` are never *displayed* outside the Overlay root, and `tell_remaining` never reaches a `PRESENTATION` subscriber (AT-16); the token lint is Attention's AC-AT-66, reused by reference. *Why:* Pillar 3 ("shown in the world, never a meter" means the meter exists only here); at:1370: the Debug Overlay and the diagnostics channel are "the one place values may be shown". *AC group:* pages (attention), gating (lint).

**DO-26. Frame page, channels and world debug draws.** *Rule:* `P-FRAME` shows the Pipeline's diagnostic surface (pipe:857). `P-CHANNELS` shows the channel list (id, owner, status `OK` / `PENDING` / `STALE` / `ERROR` / `SUSPENDED` / `REJECTED`, the same six values as the channel status machine in States and Transitions, row count) and the selected channel's rows, plus one toggle per declared world debug draw (Lighting occluder outlines and `r_lit` circles, Emitter cones, Layout rects, Horror true positions, Body sort origins, the Pipeline's own layer-0 toggles at pipe:300 row 11: sort origins, occluders, the look-ahead box, pixel grid, band indices, camera limits, the fractional-transform tree-walk). The operator moves the row cursor over the channels and the toggles with `cursor_next` / `cursor_prev`; `activate` on a channel row selects it and on a toggle row flips it (DO-8). Toggles control the dev-only debug node in layer 0 (pipe:102 note e); every `draw_line` uses a width of `OVERLAY_DEBUG_DRAW_WIDTH_PX` >= 1 base px (4.7 lines have no feather `[REF breaking-changes.md]`; pipe:923). The `DEBUG_OVERLAY` render-state modifier (pipe:267) is a flag over any state, not a state. **Risk (spike S-4):** layer 0 is the lit world viewport and the Filter's full-screen pass sits on its container (pipe PR-3, PR-10), so a debug node there can be hidden in a dark compartment, jolted by the camera and, at stage 3, graded by the sane-to-flesh hue pass; the Overlay's own world marks could be corrupted by the lie they are meant to expose. MVP keeps the locked layer-0 debug node with draws set unshaded `[UNV]`; a spike measures that draw colours are unchanged under `force_stage` 3. If it fails the fallback is a **layer-4 projection** (the Overlay draws the same outlines in the root viewport through the camera's world-to-screen transform, which the Pipeline would have to expose): a Pipeline amendment request held in Open Questions, not decided here. *Why:* pipe:102 and pipe:104 note e. *AC group:* palette, world draws.

**DO-27. Palette and severity presentation.** *Rule:* The UI palette only: text Paper White `#DDE4EA`, the perceived value Hull Grey `#8C949C`, selection Tungsten `#FFCC66`, plate Ground Graphite `#1C2128` (frame `#5C6670`). Severity is a **text tag** first (`OK`, `NOTE`, `WARN`, `DEFECT`). Alarm Red `#E0102A` appears only as a warning **mark** beside a `DEFECT` tag and for `NO_MASK` (pipe:735), never as a row fill, an accent or a decoration (ab:375 keeps red "exclusively for alarm so rarity is the signal"; ab:463 "only on a failing instrument; never decorative"; a `DEFECT` row is a failing instrument, a `NOTE` is not). `WARN` uses no red. `NOT_USED` (the `PER_ELEMENT` container state) is not red (pf:446, AC-PF-38). Never violet, never flesh, even when the world lie leaks violet (Body :853 asks the same of its debug tint: Hull Grey or a text-only cue). *Why:* Pillar 1 (the sane palette is inviolable; violet and flesh are reserved for sanity leaks). *(orchestrator ruling: Alarm Red narrowed to a mark by ab:375; an art-director-ratifiable note is carried in Open Questions.)* *AC group:* palette.

**DO-28. Legibility and refresh.** *Rule:* Text-first pages on a Graphite plate with fixed-width digits; numerals refresh at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` while markers and debug draws update per frame; a bar, where shown, is **linear with a needle mark** (no ring, no dial; the 2026-10-26 UI-grammar entry in `docs/consistency-failures.md`; ab 3.4). Pages, not floating windows. `PAUSED` freezes `T` but numerals still refresh so a paused session is inspectable. *Why:* the 10 Hz numeral cadence keeps digits readable while per-frame marks stay smooth. *AC group:* palette, pages.

**DO-29. Inertness.** *Rule:* With the Overlay `FULL` versus absent over the same scripted run: the `state_hash` sequence is identical; the Filter's draw path is unchanged (E-PF-61); the Overlay's extra `perceived` calls hit the Filter's per-frame memo and allocate nothing on the honest path; and the frame cost stays under `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US` `[PLACEHOLDER]` (dev builds only). The Overlay reuses the five dependents' absence ACs by reference and adds the accessibility-enumeration, rebinding-enumeration, screen-reader-tree and reserved-hue negatives. *Why:* observation must not change the system (AT-32, AC-AT-56 shape); inertness is the first acceptance theme. *AC group:* inertness.

**DO-30. The Never list.** *Rule:* The Overlay is never: an accessibility option, caption, setting or shortcut (R-7, ab:886); a rebinding entry; a screen-reader (AccessKit) node; a tutorial line or onboarding step; a "developer mode" menu item; an achievement or a telemetry upload; audio or sonification (Audio's voice counters are displayed, never sonified; no beep on `DEFECT`); a release-build presence; a write surface (no submit, mutator, `force_*` button, teleport, spawn, knob slider or rewind); a replay or rewind debugger (the command-log tail is display only; replay is the kernel's G3 harness); an in-game editor (anti-pillar: not a freeform builder). *Why:* guard rails in Player Fantasy; P4 ("an honest overlay on a lying HUD would make Pillar 4 switchable off"). *AC group:* gating, input path, palette.

**DO-31. Evidence capture and marks.** *Rule:* One `dump` action writes a plain-text snapshot of every page, headed by `T`, `hash_T`, the algorithm tag (the kernel's `hash_algo` field, DO-17, granted 2026-10-27; `H64 (pending ADR)` only if it is absent), the viewer and the mode, to a dev path under `user://debug/` `[PLACEHOLDER]`; the number of retained dumps is capped (`DIAG_DUMP_MAX_FILES`). Screenshots follow `.claude/docs/run-and-observe.md` into `production/qa/evidence/`. One `mark` action stamps the current `T` (up to `DIAG_MARK_MAX`) as a named observer mark; the first `DIAG_MARK_MAX` marks are kept and a further `mark` is refused with a `NOTE` rather than dropping an older stamp (*orchestrator ruling*: a stopwatch start must not vanish silently); `mark` is accepted in `OFF`, `FULL` and `OBSERVER_STRIP` and ignored in `HIDDEN`, and marks are session state that survives a mode change; the stage-entry stopwatch (`stage_entry_latency_s`, Formulas) uses it so an observer can time sanity stage-1 entry from boarding. Streaming and CSV or JSON export remain the AT-32 diagnostics-pipeline ADR. *Why:* AC-SN-88 and AC-BS-122 need retained evidence. *AC group:* evidence.

**DO-32. Settle after restore and boundaries.** *Rule:* On `LOADING` to `RUNNING` (restore, new world, transit) every history window (P99 samples, peaks, silent-lie counters, `RETURN` trackers) is cleared, and the classifier reports `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `SETTLING` for `DIAG_POST_RESTORE_SETTLE_FRAMES` rendered frames after the first `RUNNING` tick, so the Filter's presentation-local tails (E-PF-5: a return track dropped by a restore) never read as defects. Opening the Overlay mid-session starts empty windows and says "insufficient samples (n/N)" rather than issuing a verdict. *Why:* a restore legitimately produces an `ALL` collapse and drops presentation-local state. *AC group:* kernel states.

**DO-33. Kernel-side requests (routed, not edited).** *Rule:* The GDD requested the following of the kernel owner and does not depend on any of them being granted; **(a), (b) and (c) were APPLIED by the kernel owner 2026-10-27** (command-simulation-layer.md: the `diagnostics` accessor carrying `hash_T` and `hash_algo`, `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` in its Tuning Knobs, forced hashes at snapshot, restore and `PAUSED`, kern:915 re-worded, the timer read-only): (a) a read-only `diagnostics` accessor, one snapshot per tick or on request, zero allocation when the Overlay is absent, no effect on `state_hash`, **carrying `hash_T` and a `hash_algo` field** (neither is a kernel-published name today; kern names only `H64`) (P-9; kern:749 lists contents, not an API shape); (b) a kernel knob `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` (default 60, range 6 to 600), forced hashes at snapshot and restore (SL-12 already computes one) and on `PAUSED`, with the hash run outside P3 so its time never enters D.6's sum of `t_hook`, and with kern:915 amended from "refreshed for that tick" to "refreshed at the diagnostics cadence"; (c) the kernel's diagnostics timer exposed read-only for the Overlay's own cost row (H9 allow-lists only that timer and the tick driver, kern:781; until answered the self-cost AC is a manual check against the engine profiler). If the kernel declines, the Overlay shows whatever the kernel publishes. *Why:* a DIAGNOSTIC subscriber reads; it cannot schedule kernel work. kern:915 now reads "refreshed at the diagnostics cadence" (applied 2026-10-27). *(orchestrator ruling: the kernel computes, the Overlay displays.)* *AC group:* unblocked sibling ACs (kern:915).

**DO-34. Vertical Slice B contract rows only.** *Rule:* Authoritative values appear on the `HOST`; a `MIRROR` shows only its own seat's true/perceived pair, **and that own-seat pair classifies `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `NO_LEDGER`** (`sanity.ledger` is `HOST_ONLY`, so `ledger_active` cannot be evaluated: the pair is displayed, never judged `HONEST` or `DELIBERATE`); `HOST_MIRROR_MISMATCH` is defined and deferred; `remote_queries` is displayed as the leakage indicator and is expected 0 in MVP; there is no multi-seat panel design and the seat cycler is a contract only. A loopback (two local instances) stub fixture is the only MVP proof. *(provisional — Co-op Session & Replication undesigned.)* *Why:* P-6; scope guard. *AC group:* cross-system.

**The channel registration contract** (DO-12; proposed registry name `diag_panel_contract`):

| Field | Type / range | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `channel_id` | snake_case string, unique | A collision is a registration error shown on `P-CHANNELS` |
| `owner` | system name | For the channel list and the Cross-References |
| `rows[]` | ordered list of `(name, unit, kind, severity_rule)` | `kind` is one of `COUNTER` (monotonic), `GAUGE`, `FLAG` (bool), `TEXT`, `TABLE` (rows by columns, capped); `unit` is a label, never part of the name |
| `severity_rule` | predicate over the row's value, result in `OK` / `NOTE` / `WARN` / `DEFECT` | Evaluated by the Overlay; a `DEFECT` rule cites the owner's rule it enforces |
| `draws[]` | optional list of `(toggle_id, label)` | World debug-draw toggles; the owner supplies the draw callback for the dev node in layer 0 |
| `pull()` | returns a plain data snapshot, no live reference | Called at most at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`; the Overlay copies |
| limits | `DIAG_CHANNELS_MAX`, `DIAG_ROWS_PER_CHANNEL_MAX` | Exceeding either is a registration error row, not a crash |

**The classifier classes** (DO-21) (registry: `desync_classes`). Evaluation order is the row order; the first match wins; the last row is the catch-all. All inputs are read in the same frame (DO-20).

| # | Class | Severity | Test | Cross-read / note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `UNAVAILABLE` | `NOTE` | An input is missing. Reasons: `NOT_RUNNING` (kernel not `RUNNING`), `SETTLING` (DO-32), `REMOTE_VIEWER` (DO-10), `NO_LEDGER` (a mirror, DO-16), `NO_RESOLVER` (no ref resolver), `NO_TRUE_SOURCE` (`SCANNER`) | The reason is always shown; when several hold, the one listed first here wins (`NOT_RUNNING`, `SETTLING`, `REMOTE_VIEWER`, `NO_LEDGER`, `NO_RESOLVER`, `NO_TRUE_SOURCE`; *orchestrator ruling*: the most global cause first). **Never** shown as `HONEST`. |
| 2 | `SKEW` | `NOTE` | `ledger_active` XOR (`lie_id != 0` and `motion_mode != RETURN`), for at most `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES` consecutive rendered frames (`n_skew`) | Sanity and the Filter are one run apart. Renamed from `PENDING` (collides with Sanity's ledger state `PENDING`, sn:388-389, and the channel status). Independent of whether `lie_id` stays non-zero in `RETURN` (OQ-DO-2) |
| 3 | `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` | `DEFECT` | `lie_id != 0`, `motion_mode != RETURN` and `leak_modes & GLEAM == 0` (violates PF-15 and Pillar 4). The `RETURN` exclusion stops a fading return amplitude (0 at `LEAK_EPS_DEG`, pf:748) from reading as a dropped leak *(orchestrator ruling: added by the author after the checkpoint, so the row stays independent of whether `lie_id` stays non-zero in `RETURN`; OQ-DO-2)* | `leak_modes_dropped`, `leak_repaired` |
| 4 | `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` | `DEFECT` | `ledger_active` and either (a) `lie_id != 0`, the lie age `run_now - active_run` is at least `ceil(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x (end_run - active_run))` runs (binary-frame refs: `SWAP_LEAD_S` x 5 runs in place of the reach age), and `disc <= tol_eff` for more than `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES` consecutive rendered frames counted from the reach age (`n_eq`); or (b) `lie_id == 0` for more than `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES` (the Filter never picked the entry up) | `lie_unrenderable`, `lies_partial`, `lie_short_reach`. The age gate is why a slow `GLIDE` onset (departure rate as low as 0.3 to 0.5 deg/s) is never a false defect; `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` 0.6 `[PLACEHOLDER]` is the Filter's (pf D.2), cited, and by that age arrival at `D_eff >= D_VIS_MIN_DEG` is guaranteed (pf:646) |
| 5 | `DEFECT_BYPASS` | `DEFECT` | The probe is present and `abs(drawn - p.displayed_value)` exceeds `LEAK_EPS_DEG` in needle-angle space (DO-22) | With no probe, `bypass_check` = `ABSENT`; never clean |
| 6 | `DELIBERATE` | `NOTE`, tagged `LIE` | `ledger_active`, `lie_id != 0`, `leak_modes & GLEAM != 0`, and not row 4; tagged `ONSET` while the lie age is below the row-4 age gate or `disc <= tol_eff` inside the grace | Shows `lie_kind`, `motion_mode`, `leak_amplitude_pm` |
| 7 | `RETURNING` | `NOTE` | `motion_mode == RETURN`, not `ledger_active` (no `lie_id` test) and `disc != 0`, `t_ret <= t_return_max(lie)` (published by the Filter per lie, registry `filter_counters`; depends on `rm`), and the two-phase closure test of Formulas: the envelope for `t_ret < WANDER_BLEND_S`, the step test after it. On a binary-frame ref any non-zero `disc` fails this row | Closes to `HONEST` on the frame `disc == 0`, even if `motion_mode` is still `RETURN` and whether or not `lie_id` has cleared (row 8). The Filter's own closure (pf:291, CLEAR): `motion_mode` returns to HONEST without an event once the discrepancy falls under `LEAK_EPS_DEG`, and the honest path is then exact, so the return ends at a sub-`LEAK_EPS_DEG` discrepancy and then switches to `displayed == tv` (OQ-DO-20 answered). *(orchestrator ruling: DO-21 reconciliation 1; closure rule added at final authoring after the qa-lead's review found that row 7, as drafted, would have kept matching a closed return.)* |
| 8 | `HONEST` | `OK` | Not `ledger_active`, (`lie_id == 0` or `motion_mode == RETURN`) and `p.displayed_value == tv` exactly. The `RETURN` clause keeps the row independent of whether `lie_id` stays non-zero until the follower closes (OQ-DO-2) | The Filter's counters are all 0 |
| 9 | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` | `DEFECT` | The catch-all: not `ledger_active`, `lie_id == 0` and `disc != 0` outside a valid `RETURN`; `lie_id != 0`, `motion_mode != RETURN`, not `ledger_active`, beyond the skew grace; a `RETURN` that fails row 7 (past `t_return_max`, a growing discrepancy, a non-zero `disc` on a binary-frame ref) | A Filter or Instruments bug, or real desync |

**The seven pages as data tables** (DO-15). Cadence key: `tick` = collected in the handler every kernel tick and peak-held; `R` = refreshed at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`; `ev` = on event. "Kernel dx" is the kernel's diagnostics accessor (P-9; the shape is the kernel's or the ADR's, so the labels here are display labels, not API names). Severity rules marked `[PLACEHOLDER]` await their owner's confirmation.

*P-KERNEL*

| Row | Source | Unit | Cadence | Severity rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | kernel dx | ticks, and mm:ss at `TICK_RATE` 60 | tick, R | none |
| Kernel state | kernel dx | enum (`BOOTING`, `LOADING`, `RUNNING`, `PAUSED`, `SAVING`, `FAULTED`) | ev | `FAULTED` = `DEFECT`; others `OK` |
| Queue depth, total | kernel dx | commands against `QUEUE_CAPACITY` 1024 | tick (peak) | `queue_load_pm` at or above `DIAG_QUEUE_WARN_PM` = `WARN` |
| Queue depth, per issuer | kernel dx | commands against `PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY` 256 | tick (peak) | same rule on the worst issuer |
| Command log tail | kernel dx | rows `(tick, issuer, type, result, reject reason)`; `DIAG_CMD_LOG_ROWS` rows | R | rejects `NOTE`; a `QUEUE_FULL` reject `WARN` |
| `log_coverage_ticks` | kernel D.13, cited | ticks, and seconds (`COMMAND_LOG_CAPACITY` 1024) | R | none; the cited worst case is at least 8 ticks |
| Rejection counters by reason | kernel dx | counts | R | a rising count `NOTE` |
| Catch-up lag (`lag_ticks`, kern:351) | kernel dx | ticks against `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4 | tick (peak) | `lag_ticks` at or above `DIAG_LAG_WARN_TICKS` = `WARN` |
| FAULT record | kernel dx | `(T, type, issuer, host_order, block_id, assertion)` | ev | present = `DEFECT` |
| Boot progress and failure | kernel dx | text | ev | a failure = `DEFECT` |

*P-HOOKS*

| Row | Source | Unit | Cadence | Severity rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Resolved order | kernel dx (kern:607) | rows `(index, hook_id, owner, divisor, offset, after)` | ev | none; an unknown id is a boot `DEFECT` |
| Per-hook `t_hook`, last and window maximum | kernel dx | ms | tick (peak) | none (the kernel owns the threshold) |
| Sum of running `t_hook` this tick and `step_over_budget` | kernel D.6, cited | ms; bool | tick | over = `WARN` |
| Over-budget ticks in the window | model count | ticks | R | more than 0 = `NOTE` |
| `graph_hook_sum_run`, last | `hook_window_p99` formulas | ms | per Graph run | none |
| `graph_hook_sum_run`, P99 and maximum over `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` | same | ms against `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` | R | P99 above 0.5 or maximum above 1.0 (MVP) = `WARN`, tagged "dev template: indicative"; fewer than `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` shows "insufficient samples (n/N)" |
| Hook rate | kernel D.2, cited | Hz = `TICK_RATE / divisor` | ev | none |

*P-STATE*

| Row | Source | Unit | Cadence | Severity rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `state_hash`, `hash_T`, age, algorithm tag (the kernel's `hash_algo` field, DO-33a, granted 2026-10-27; `H64 (pending ADR)` only if the field is absent) | kernel dx (D.12) | u64 hex; ticks | R | age: `NOTE` beyond 2x `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` (the kernel's published hash cadence, accepted 2026-10-27, DO-33b); an unknown `hash_algo` = `WARN` |
| Per-block table | block registry (CR-8) | `block_id`, owner, class, `schema_version`, persist scope | ev | none |
| Per-block hash | owner `block_hash()`; `n/a` otherwise; the K-1 column if adopted | u64 hex, with the tick it was taken | R | `n/a` = `NOTE`, not `DEFECT` |
| Dirty keys per block | P5 batch | keys; `dirty_load_pm` | tick (peak) | `WARN` at or above `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` |
| `ALL` collapses | P5 batch | count | tick | more than 0 = `WARN`, annotated `post_restore` when one follows a restore |
| `HASH_DRIFT` | Save/Load SL-12 compare, G4 harness | enum; first differing `block_id` | ev | drift = `DEFECT` |
| `PERCEPTION_IN_HASH` | test-harness result | enum; `n/a` live | ev | true = `DEFECT` |
| Save status | Save/Load channel (`save_status`, last generation, `written_at_T`, last `SL_*` reason) | enum | ev | `FAILED` = `DEFECT` |
| Graph numeric-tier counters (`graph_saturations[field]`, incl. `DT_CLAMP`) and the other `graph_*` counters | Graph diagnostics channel (lsg:735; LS-24) | counts per field | R | any field above 0 = `WARN` (LS-24: numeric soft invariant, "every test suite asserts `graph_saturations` is all zeros") |

*P-TRUTH* (rows as DO-20; per selected ref)

| Row | Source | Unit | Cadence | Severity rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| True value | the owner's `true_value` (Graph, Suit) | the owner's unit | R | none |
| Perceived `displayed_value`, `displayed_pm` | `perceived(ref, viewer)` | the owner's unit; per mille | R | none |
| `lie_id`, `lie_kind`, `leak_modes`, `leak_amplitude_pm`, `motion_mode` | the `Perceived` record | enums; per mille | R | none |
| Ledger `start_run`, `end_run`, state | `sanity.ledger` | runs | R | none |
| `disc`, `tol_eff`, `ledger_active`, `t_ret` and `t_return_max` while `RETURN`, class | model (`desync_classifier`) | the owner's unit | R | the class severity (class table) |
| Drawn value | Instruments probe (P-2), optional | needle angle | frame | `DEFECT_BYPASS` |
