| The 14 Filter counters (PF-41, registry `filter_counters`): `lies_drawn`, `leak_modes_dropped`, `leak_repaired`, `lies_partial`, `lie_short_reach`, `lie_unrenderable`, `lie_collisions`, `remote_queries`, `filter_route`, `filter_pass_disabled`, `first_frame_filtered`, `tag_defaults_applied`, `filter_draws_dropped`, `render_cpu_us` | Filter dev surface (PF-41) | counts; `render_cpu_us` in us; `filter_route` enum; `filter_pass_disabled` enum or bool | R | `lies_drawn` above 0 = `NOTE`. `filter_pass_disabled` = `NO_MASK` is `DEFECT` with the red mark (pipe:735, pipe:991); any other disabled reason (true) = `NOTE`; false = `OK`. `filter_route` container state `NOT_USED` = `OK` (not red, pf:446, AC-PF-38). `lies_partial`, `lie_short_reach`, `lie_unrenderable`, `filter_draws_dropped` above 0 = `WARN`. `leak_repaired` above 0 = `DEFECT` (the Filter's severity, registry `filter_counters`); `lie_collisions`, `leak_modes_dropped`, `first_frame_filtered`, `tag_defaults_applied` above 0 = `NOTE`. `remote_queries` above 0 = `NOTE` in MVP (it is the leakage indicator, DO-34). `render_cpu_us` is compared as a **rolling median over `DIAG_FILTER_CPU_WINDOW_FRAMES` frames** against the registry's `CPU_BUDGET_MS` 0.5 (a median per frame, AC-PF-43b): median above 0.5 = `WARN`, never a per-frame verdict. **All counter severities are the Filter's own (registry `filter_counters`)** (OQ-DO-2 resolved 2026-10-27; every counter not named above = `NOTE`): PF-41 names the counters, not their severity. |

*P-SANITY* (DO-24; rows per `PlayerId`; sources `sanity.state`, `sanity_view(player_id)`, `sanity.ledger`)

| Row | Source | Unit | Cadence | Severity rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Level, `stage_dwell` | `sanity.state` | mSU; runs | R | none |
| `stage`, `tell_on`, `harm_eligible` | `sanity_view(player_id)` | enums; bool | R | none |
| Ledger `lies[]` | `sanity.ledger` (sn:170) | rows `{slot, subject_ref, kind, params, state, onset_stage, start_run, active_run, end_run, debunked_run, leak_modes, first_lie}` (`active_run = start_run + delay_runs`, `end_run = active_run + life_runs`, SN-16); `state` shows `PENDING`, `ACTIVE`, `EXPIRED`, `DEBUNKED`, `INVALIDATED`, and only `ACTIVE` is `ledger_active` (DO-21) | R | none |
| `immune[]` | `sanity.ledger` | rows | R | none |
| Telemetry counters | Sanity (SN-28): `eligible_entries_per_min`, lies shown, acted on, debunked, expired unseen | counts; per minute | R | none (Sanity owns its invariants) |
| `FORCED` tag | `sanity.state` `forced` (per-seat dev diagnostic flag, SN-28; set by `force_stage` / `force_level`, cleared only by NEW_GAME, always false in a release export) | tag | ev | read each refresh; a forced seat = `NOTE` |
| Stage-entry stopwatch | `stage_entry_latency_s` (observer `mark`) | seconds | ev | none (Sanity's AC owns the 268 to 363 s window) |

### States and Transitions

The Overlay has no simulation state and no kernel state of its own (DO-9). It has three dev-session machines: the **mode machine** (what is drawn), the **channel status machine** (what each registered channel reports), and a **behaviour overlay** keyed to the kernel's state (what the Overlay does in each of the kernel's six states). None is a block, none enters a snapshot or `state_hash`, and no transition submits a command or changes `T`.

**1. Mode machine** (DO-6 to DO-8). Who may cause a transition: only the operator, through the Input Adapter's forwarded dev actions (DO-8), or the composition root at launch (the launch option). Nothing in the simulation, the Filter, Sanity or any sibling can change the mode.

| State | Subscribed | Collecting | Draws | Entry | Exit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `OFF` | yes (registered) | no: the handler returns at once | nothing | Dev launch default (`OVERLAY_DEFAULT_MODE`); `toggle` from `FULL` or `OBSERVER_STRIP` | `toggle` to `FULL` | The only per-tick cost is one early-out call. Histories are cleared on entry. |
| `FULL` | yes | yes | pages `P-KERNEL` to `P-FRAME`, then `P-CHANNELS` | `toggle` from `OFF` (the only way in) | `toggle` to `OFF`; `mode` to `OBSERVER_STRIP` | The tester-hostile mode (DO-7). Page changes and the selection actions (`cursor_next`, `cursor_prev`, `activate`) act only here. |
| `OBSERVER_STRIP` | yes | yes | one text line (DO-7) | `mode` from `FULL`; the `reveal` chord from `HIDDEN` | `toggle` to `OFF`; `mode` to `HIDDEN` | Shows no ref and no ledger field, but names the stage and phase, so it is tester-hostile too. |
| `HIDDEN` | yes | yes | nothing | `mode` from `OBSERVER_STRIP`; launch option `--overlay=hidden` (placeholder) | The `reveal` chord only | Collecting, so the observer sees full windows on reveal. Plain actions are ignored. |

| From | Action | To | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| `OFF` | `toggle` | `FULL` | The dev tag is present (otherwise the action does not exist) |
| `FULL`, `OBSERVER_STRIP` | `toggle` | `OFF` | none |
| `FULL` | `mode` | `OBSERVER_STRIP` | none |
| `OBSERVER_STRIP` | `mode` | `HIDDEN` | none |
| `HIDDEN` | `mode`, `toggle`, `page_next`, `page_prev`, `dump`, `mark`, `cursor_next`, `cursor_prev`, `activate` | unchanged (ignored) | Plain actions do nothing in `HIDDEN` so a tester cannot expose or stamp anything |
| `HIDDEN` | `reveal` chord | `OBSERVER_STRIP` | Reveal goes to the strip, not `FULL`; a second deliberate `mode` is needed to show a lie |
| `FULL` | `page_next`, `page_prev` | same mode, adjacent page | Fixed page order, wraps |
| `FULL` | `cursor_next`, `cursor_prev`, `activate` | unchanged | `HIDDEN` and `OBSERVER_STRIP` ignore them |
| any visible mode | `dump` | unchanged | Writes the text snapshot (DO-31); from `OBSERVER_STRIP` it dumps every page, not just the strip |
| any | `mark` | unchanged | Stamps `T` as an observer mark, up to `DIAG_MARK_MAX` (`HIDDEN` ignores it) |
| any | kernel enters `FAULTED` in a dev build | unchanged | Forces the FAULT record onto the strip and, in `FULL`, onto `P-KERNEL`; `HIDDEN` stays hidden (the tester must not see it either) |
| any | release export | not reachable | The root is absent (DO-2); the actions are not registered |

*(Design note: the `mode` cycle runs `FULL` to `OBSERVER_STRIP` to `HIDDEN` and stops there; `HIDDEN` leaves only by the chord, and the chord leads only to the strip. `FULL` is entered only from `OFF` by `toggle`, so returning from the strip to the full pages takes two deliberate actions (`toggle` to `OFF`, `toggle` to `FULL`). `OFF` is distinct from `HIDDEN` because `OFF` stops collection and clears history, whereas `HIDDEN` keeps windows full for the observer.)*

**2. Channel status machine** (DO-12, DO-14). Who may cause a transition: the owner's registration (at composition-root time), the owner's `pull()` results, and the Overlay's own fault isolation. The Overlay shows the status on `P-CHANNELS`.

| Status | Entry | Exit | Meaning and display |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PENDING` | The channel is not registered when first drawn (a late registration) | A successful registration and first `pull()` | Shown as `PENDING`, severity `NOTE`; the page does not wait |
| `OK` | A successful `pull()` within `DIAG_CHANNEL_STALE_S` | A `pull()` error; no update for `DIAG_CHANNEL_STALE_S` | The rows are evaluated by their declared `severity_rule` |
| `STALE` | No update for `DIAG_CHANNEL_STALE_S` | A successful `pull()` | Shown as `STALE`, severity `NOTE`; the last values stay visible, marked |
| `ERROR` | A `pull()` or page render throws | A successful `pull()`; or `SUSPENDED` after `DIAG_PANEL_ERR_LATCH_N` consecutive failures | One `DEFECT` row names the `channel_id` and the error class (the kernel isolates a throwing subscriber, kern:867; this is the inner layer) |
| `SUSPENDED` | `DIAG_PANEL_ERR_LATCH_N` consecutive failures | The next mode change | Not pulled; the row stays a `DEFECT` so the failure cannot go unnoticed |
| `REJECTED` | A registration error: duplicate `channel_id`, `DIAG_CHANNELS_MAX` or `DIAG_ROWS_PER_CHANNEL_MAX` exceeded | A corrected registration at the next launch | A registration error row, never a crash |

**3. Behaviour per kernel state** (DO-11). The kernel's six states are the kernel's (kern:151); the table says what the Overlay does in each. Nothing the Overlay does changes a kernel state.

| Kernel state | Model input | Draws | Classifier | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BOOTING` | Boot progress; on failure the unknown `hook_id` or the cycle path (kern:606); no sim read views yet | The boot-progress row; on failure the failure row | `UNAVAILABLE` / `NOT_RUNNING` | A channel registered late shows `PENDING` (E-DO). Channel registration is accepted only while the kernel is `BOOTING`, a rule this GDD adopts by analogy with Kernel CR-6, which itself covers blocks, command types and hooks only, so a channel that misses it never appears. |
| `LOADING` | Kernel progress only; sim read views are not read | A `LOADING` banner over the frozen last page | `UNAVAILABLE` / `NOT_RUNNING` | Histories are cleared at the exit edge (DO-32) |
| `RUNNING` | All views and diagnostics | All pages | Live | The reference state |
| `PAUSED` | Frozen `T`; views stable | All pages; numerals still refresh | Live (values stable) | The kernel runs a paused service cycle (P1, P2, P4, P5, P6 with no P3, `T` unchanged, CR-16) for non-diegetic commands, so P5 batches can still arrive with `T` unchanged: the Overlay counts them but folds no hook timing. The kernel forces a hash on pause (DO-33). |
| `SAVING` | All; `save_status` `WRITING` / `WRITTEN` | All pages plus the Save/Load channel's save row | Live | The save row is the Save/Load channel's (`save_status`, last generation, `written_at_T`; sl:405-407) |
| `FAULTED` (dev build) | The FAULT record `(T, type, issuer, host_order, block_id, assertion)` and the last good views | The FAULT record pinned; other pages frozen | `UNAVAILABLE` / `NOT_RUNNING` | The kernel is not ticking; the render loop and the Overlay keep running (chart-table-web-map-and-menus.md:377 accepted as written). In a release build the kernel returns to the menu and the Overlay does not exist. |

**4. Per-reference classifier state** (DO-21). A reference's class is recomputed at each refresh and has no memory except three counters the model keeps per `(ref, viewer)`: `n_eq` (consecutive rendered frames with `disc <= tol_eff` since the age gate opened: 0 until `lie_age >= reach_age`), `n_skew` (consecutive rendered frames the ledger and the `Perceived` record disagree on a lie's presence, `ledger_active` XOR `lie_id != 0` while `motion_mode != RETURN`) and `t_ret` (time since `RETURN` began), plus two values it latches from the owners rather than counts: the lie age `run_now - active_run` read off the ledger row, and `disc_end` / `tv_end` captured on the first frame of `RETURN` (the envelope's anchor). They reset on a restore (DO-32), on a viewer switch, on a mode change from `OFF`, and when a lie ends; they freeze while the kernel is `PAUSED` (they count rendered frames, but a paused session must not accrue silent-lie time). Typical class progressions, each a sequence of classes over refreshes, are:

| Situation | Progression |
|---|---|
| A fair lie begins and ends | `HONEST` -> `SKEW` (a few frames; Sanity's own `PENDING` row is not `ledger_active`, so the 2 to 5 s onset delay shows as `HONEST`) -> `DELIBERATE` (`ONSET` while the lie age is below the reach age or `disc` is still inside `tol_eff`) -> `DELIBERATE` -> (entry removed) `RETURNING` -> `HONEST` |
| A lie that never renders | `HONEST` -> `SKEW` -> `DELIBERATE` (`ONSET`) -> `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` once the lie age reaches `ceil(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x (end_run - active_run))` runs **and** `n_eq` passes `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES` |
| A stuck return | `RETURNING` -> `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` when `t_ret` passes `t_return_max(lie)` or the discrepancy grows past the envelope or the step bound |
| A restore with the Overlay open | any -> `UNAVAILABLE` / `SETTLING` for `DIAG_POST_RESTORE_SETTLE_FRAMES` -> live |

### Interactions with Other Systems

**The Overlay registers no hook, owns no block, exposes no mutator, no command type, no `read_view` and no dirty key of its own, so it fixes no interface name in any registry.** Every interface below belongs to the other side; "data in" is what the Overlay reads, "data out" is what it gives back (a channel registration, a debug-draw toggle, a request) and never a write to the simulation. There are **no downstream dependents among the systems designed in this run**: the roughly seventeen siblings that name the Overlay are the *owners* of what it reads, and the tables below are the Overlay's bidirectional row for each of them. Disk status was checked with Glob on `design/gdd/` at authoring (25 files): every system named below as "designed" is on disk; HUD & Suit Layer is designed (Status Designed, on disk); In-World Instruments & Inspect View is designed (this run) (Status Designed, on disk); Accessibility Settings, Settings & Input Rebinding, Onboarding, Co-op Session & Replication, Crew Strip, Chat/Voice/Pings, the dev console and every ADR have no file.

**A. Upstream dependencies (the three in the index), with the dependency GDD's own row quoted**

| System (status) | The dependency GDD's own row, quoted | Data in | Data out | Interface owner | Consistency finding |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (hard; designed) | kern:203 row 11: "Subscriptions only: Filter as `PRESENTATION`, Overlay as `DIAGNOSTIC` \| Read views (`SIM` + `PERCEPTION_OUT`); diagnostics: tick, kernel state, queue depth, last-N command log with results, hook order and timings, rejection counters, state hash \| Kernel: views and diagnostics surface · Filter/Overlay: drawing \| Neither gets submit or mutators. Overlay absent from release builds (never an accessibility feature)." And kern:690 (Dependencies): "Debug Overlay (True Values) \| Hard \| `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription \| Reads only". And kern:749 (UI Requirements) lists the developer-only surface. | `T`, kernel state, total and per-issuer queue depth, the command log tail with results and reject reasons, the resolved hook order with `t_hook` and the `step_over_budget` flag (D.6), rejection counters by reason, the catch-up lag counter, `state_hash` of the committed tick (D.12; the kernel names the algorithm `H64`, kern:531), P5 batches `(block_id, tick, change_keys)` (CR-10), the block registry (CR-8), the FAULT record, `log_coverage_ticks` (D.13), `lag_ticks` (kern:351) | Nothing is written. Overlay: drawing | **Consistent** on class, direction and absence from release. **Amendments:** (1) kern:915 now reads "refreshed at the diagnostics cadence" (APPLIED 2026-10-27); (2) kern:749 lists contents, not an API shape (P-9), still open; (3) the diagnostics timer is exposed read-only (APPLIED 2026-10-27, DO-33c). |

Requests (answered by the Filter, registry `filter_counters` and `perceived_query`: `t_return_max` per lie and the PF-45 calibration on its dev channel; per-counter severities; `lie_id` is kept while `motion_mode` is `RETURN` and is 0 once the follower closes, OQ-DO-2 (v)); publish a true source for `SCANNER` and confirm the ref-to-subject map (PF-9, provisional) | Filter: query and counters. Overlay: shows both, classifies, never computes a leak | **Consistent.** The Filter's own rows (pf:558, pf:1154) still read "undesigned-provisional" and must be flipped to "designed" when this GDD lands (OQ-PF-29 is the owner's re-check). The index row for this system (idx:98) now lists the Pipeline among its dependencies, so the edge has been added, and this GDD records it as soft. `diag.*` names stay *provisional* (pipe:1055) until their owners freeze them. |

## Formulas

**Scope and determinism.** The Overlay defines **nine** formula blocks (the ruling's eight formulas, with `graph_hook_sum_run` and `hook_window_p99` registered separately), and all nine are display arithmetic: none is hashed, replicated, saved or read by a simulation system, and none can change `state_hash` (DO-9). Where the inputs are integers (every Graph, Suit and kernel quantity is an integer unit, `mC`, `mGU`, `dkPa`, `permille`, `bp`, ticks, runs, microseconds) the arithmetic is integer, with explicit floor and ceiling, so the table-driven tests of the classifier and the percentile are exact and identical on every machine. Where a locked formula belongs to another GDD the Overlay **cites it by name and never re-expresses it with other numbers**: `step_over_budget` and `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 ("a threshold, not a cap", kern D.6), `state_hash` (kern D.12), `log_coverage_ticks` (kern D.13, `COMMAND_LOG_CAPACITY` 1024), `lag_ticks` (kern:351, repeated only in the form the kernel publishes it), and the Filter's return path (pf D.6). Every `DIAG_*` / `OVERLAY_*` value below is a `[PLACEHOLDER]` with a range in Tuning Knobs. Proposed registry names for the merge stage (written from this final text, never from a draft): `desync_classifier`, `graph_hook_sum_run`, `hook_window_p99`, `dirty_load_pm`, `queue_load_pm`, `ticks_per_overlay_refresh`, `overlay_cpu_us`, `hash_cost_per_s`, `stage_entry_latency_s`. The nine blocks below are the ruling's eight formulas (R30's six, plus `queue_load_pm`, plus `stage_entry_latency_s`), with the pair "`graph_hook_sum_run` and `hook_window_p99`" written as two blocks (F-2, F-3) because the registry names them separately.

### F-1. `desync_classifier`

The `desync_classifier` formula is defined as:

`class(ref, viewer) = first_match(row_1 ... row_9 of the DO-21 class table)`, evaluated at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` from one same-frame read of `(tv, p, ledger row, Filter counters)`

The sub-expressions the rows use (all integers in the **owner's unit** of the ref: `mC`, `mGU`, `dkPa`, `permille`, `bp`; binary-frame refs compare as 0 or 1):

```
p_int         = round_half_up(p.displayed_value)               # honest path: p.displayed_value == tv exactly, no rounding needed
disc          = abs(p_int - tv)                                 # binary-frame refs: 0 if equal else 1
q_display     = ceil((value_hi - value_lo) / 1000)              # owner's units, >= 1; from the PF-45 calibration (published by the Filter, registry `filter_counters`)
tol_eff       = max(tol(kind), q_display)                       # if q_display is unpublished: tol_eff = tol(kind)
step_enabled  = (q_display is published) or (tol(kind) > 0)     # kinds with tol 0 and no q_display skip the step sub-test
ledger_active = exists row in sanity.ledger.lies[] with row.viewer == viewer
                  and row.subject_ref == subject_of(ref) and row.state == ACTIVE          # lowest (start_run, slot) wins (PF-46)
lie_age       = run_now - active_run                            # runs since activation; Sanity runs at TICK_RATE / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR = 60 / 12 = 5 per s
reach_age     = ceil_div(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC_pm * (end_run - active_run), 1000)    # GLIDE_REACH_FRAC 0.6 -> 600 pm; binary-frame refs: SWAP_LEAD_S * 5
n_eq          = consecutive rendered frames with disc <= tol_eff, counted only from the first frame on which lie_age >= reach_age (0 before it; binary-frame refs: from SWAP_LEAD_S x 5 runs)
n_skew        = consecutive rendered frames with  ledger_active XOR (lie_id != 0 and motion_mode != RETURN)
t_ret         = (T - T_ret_start) / TICK_RATE  seconds         # ticks, not frames: freezes when the kernel is PAUSED
closing(t)    = if binary_frame(ref):               disc == 0
                elif t_ret <  WANDER_BLEND_S:       disc_t <= disc_end + abs(tv_t - tv_end) + tol_eff               # envelope
                elif step_enabled:                  disc_t <= disc_prev + abs(tv_t - tv_prev) + tol_eff             # step test, at refresh cadence
                else:                               true                                                            # held by t_return_max only
row_1  UNAVAILABLE        if any input is missing (reasons: NOT_RUNNING, SETTLING, REMOTE_VIEWER, NO_LEDGER, NO_RESOLVER, NO_TRUE_SOURCE)
row_2  SKEW               if n_skew >= 1 and n_skew <= DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES
row_3  DEFECT_NO_LEAK     if lie_id != 0 and motion_mode != RETURN and (leak_modes & GLEAM) == 0
row_4  DEFECT_SILENT_LIE  if ledger_active and ( (lie_id != 0 and lie_age >= reach_age and n_eq > DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES)
                                                 or (lie_id == 0 and n_skew > DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES) )
row_5  DEFECT_BYPASS      if probe_present and abs(drawn - angle(p.displayed_value)) > LEAK_EPS_DEG   # needle-angle space only
row_6  DELIBERATE         if ledger_active and lie_id != 0 and (leak_modes & GLEAM) != 0           # (not row_4); tag ONSET while lie_age < reach_age or n_eq <= grace
row_7  RETURNING          if motion_mode == RETURN and not ledger_active and disc != 0 and t_ret <= t_return_max(lie) and closing(t)
row_8  HONEST             if not ledger_active and (lie_id == 0 or motion_mode == RETURN) and p.displayed_value == tv   # exact equality, never tol; a closed return (disc == 0) is HONEST
row_9  DEFECT_UNCAUSED    otherwise                                                                  # the catch-all: no input falls through
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tv` | int, owner's unit | the owner's range (Graph `hp` 0 to 10000 bp; compartment `T` in mC; `P` in dkPa) | Graph `true_value(kind, id)` or Suit `true_value(key, player_id)` | The authoritative value, read first in the refresh (DO-20) |
| `p.displayed_value` | owner's unit (derived from integer `displayed_pm` 0..1000) | the instrument range | `perceived(ref, viewer)` (PF-7, PF-8), a hit on the Filter's per-frame memo | The perceived value; equals `tv` exactly on the honest path (pf:247) |
| `lie_id`, `leak_modes`, `motion_mode` | int, bitmask {GLEAM 1, GLIDE 2, SOUND 4}, enum {HONEST, GLIDE, TICK, STALE, RETURN} | per PF-8 | the `Perceived` record | The Filter's report of whether it drew a lie and how it leaks |
| `ledger_active` | bool | true / false | `sanity.ledger` (HOST_ONLY), joined by `(viewer, subject_ref)` and never by `lie_id` | Only a row in state `ACTIVE`; Sanity `PENDING` and the ended states are not active (DO-21) |
| `start_run`, `active_run`, `end_run`, `run_now` | int runs | 0 to run length | `sanity.ledger` (sn:170); Sanity's run counter | The ledger's lie window, measured from activation (`active_run`; `start_run` is used only in the `(start_run, slot)` tie-break); one run = `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12 ticks = 0.2 s |
| `tol(kind)` | int, owner's unit | 0 to 1000 | The owner's dirty-key quantum: Graph LS-20 (lsg:201) `T` 1000 mC, `P` 10 dkPa, `p_o2` 5 dkPa, store 20 permille, intensity 100 permille, `hp` 250 bp; Suit vitals buckets (suit-and-equipment.md:270-277 (SE-38)) 5 percent of maximum for reserve and cell, `integrity_bp` 250 bp; `o2_pm`, `n_o2`, `n_inert` have none (0) | Used only by rows 4 and 7; never by row 8 |
| `value_lo`, `value_hi` | int, owner's unit | per ref | Filter PF-45 calibration (published by the Filter (registry `filter_counters`) on its dev channel) | Set `q_display`; `displayed_value` is quantised to one per mille of this range |
| `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` | float | 0.6 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, 0.4 to 0.8 | Perception Filter, cited (pf D.2) | The fraction of a lie's life by which its departure has completed |
| `SWAP_LEAD_S`, `WANDER_BLEND_S` | float s | 3 and 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Perception Filter, cited: `SWAP_LEAD_S` (pf:1214), `WANDER_BLEND_S` (pf:1186) | The binary-frame gleam lead; the wander fade-out into `RETURN` |
| `t_return_max(lie)` | float s | 0 to the Filter's bound (32 s at `rm` 1.0; 53.3 s at `rm` 0.6, for the 96-deg cap) | **Published by the Filter (registry `filter_counters`)** per lie; depends on `rm`, latched per lie (E-PF-43) | The longest an honest return may take; never a constant of this GDD |
| `disc_end`, `tv_end`, `disc_prev`, `tv_prev` | int, owner's unit | per ref | Latched by the model on the first `RETURN` frame and at each refresh | The envelope anchor and the step reference |
| `p_int` | int, owner's unit | the instrument range | model | `round_half_up(p.displayed_value)`; equals `tv` on the honest path |
| `disc` | int, owner's unit | 0 to the instrument range (0 or 1 for binary-frame refs) | model | `abs(p_int - tv)` |
| `q_display` | int, owner's unit | >= 1 | model, from the Filter PF-45 calibration (published by the Filter, registry `filter_counters`) | `ceil((value_hi - value_lo) / 1000)`; the display quantum |
| `tol_eff` | int, owner's unit | >= `tol(kind)` | model | `max(tol(kind), q_display)`; `tol(kind)` when `q_display` is unpublished |
| `step_enabled` | bool | true / false | model | `(q_display is published) or (tol(kind) > 0)`; false skips the step sub-test of `closing(t)` |
| `lie_age` | int runs | 0 to unbounded (the ledger window is `end_run - active_run`) | model | `run_now - active_run`; one run = 12 ticks = 0.2 s |
| `reach_age` | int runs | 0 to 0.8 x the lie life | model | `ceil_div(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC_pm x (end_run - active_run), 1000)`; binary-frame refs: `SWAP_LEAD_S` x 5 |
| `n_eq`, `n_skew` | int rendered frames | 0 to unbounded | model | Consecutive-frame counters: `n_eq` counts `disc <= tol_eff` only from the frame `lie_age >= reach_age`; `n_skew` counts the ledger / `Perceived` presence disagreement |
| `t_ret` | float s | 0 to `t_return_max` (past it the row fails) | model, kernel ticks / `TICK_RATE` | Time since `RETURN` began; freezes while the kernel is `PAUSED` |
| `angle(v)` | float deg | -arc_deg/2 .. +arc_deg/2 | model, from the instrument's `arc_deg` (240, in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:201) and `value_lo`/`value_hi` (IW-9, :222-227), mapped as IW's `theta(pm)` (:553) with `pm = 1000 x (v - value_lo) / (value_hi - value_lo)` | The needle angle a value maps to; row 5 compares in this space only |
| `drawn`, `probe_present` | needle angle (deg), bool | per instrument; true / false | Instruments IW-38 probe (optional) | The value the node actually drew this frame and whether the render probe exists; row 5 only |
| `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES`, `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES` | int rendered frames | 120 and 24 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Tuning Knobs | Slack after the age gate; the Sanity/Filter one-run skew allowance |
| `LEAK_EPS_DEG` | float deg | 0.25 | registry `lie_leak_amplitude`, cited, **never restated** | Used only in row 5 (needle-angle space) |

**Output Range:** exactly one of nine classes per `(ref, viewer)` per refresh: `UNAVAILABLE`, `SKEW`, `DEFECT_NO_LEAK`, `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE`, `DEFECT_BYPASS`, `DELIBERATE`, `RETURNING`, `HONEST`, `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`, with severities `NOTE`, `NOTE`, `DEFECT`, `DEFECT`, `DEFECT`, `NOTE` (tagged `LIE`), `NOTE`, `OK`, `DEFECT`. Row 1 is first and row 9 is the explicit catch-all, so the function is total: every input combination, including an active ledger row with `lie_id` 0 and a `RETURN` that fails its test, maps to one class. Boundary rules: `disc == tol_eff` is **inside** the tolerance (the silent-lie counter keeps running, the step test passes); `HONEST` is **exact** equality; `t_ret == t_return_max` is inside the bound. Nothing about the function is hashed or replicated.

**Example (Graph `hp`, `tol` 250 bp, `q_display` = ceil(10000 / 1000) = 10 bp so `tol_eff` = 250, `HP_FAIL` 3000):** the table is the fixture set, one row per class, plus the `SKEW` boundary, the Sanity `PENDING` case and the `UNAVAILABLE` reasons. The `hp` dial is a synthetic fixture: Instruments holds `strain` (hp) out of the MVP manifest (in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:227), so the MVP live refs are `p_o2`, `P`, `T`, `stock`, `load`:

| # | Setup | Inputs | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Honest compartment `P` | `tv` 1013 dkPa, displayed 1013, `lie_id` 0, not `ledger_active` | `HONEST` OK |
| 2 | Exactness | `tv` 21000 mC, displayed 21001 (1 mC off, far inside `tol` 1000), `lie_id` 0 | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` DEFECT (proves row 8 is exact equality, not `tol`) |
| 3 | `FALSE_OK` on a failed node, onset | `tv` hp 2400, displayed 2650 (`disc` 250, inside `tol_eff`), `ledger_active`, `lie_id` 7, GLEAM + GLIDE (3), `lie_age` 40 runs < `reach_age` 120 | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE`, tagged `ONSET` |
| 4 | Same lie, established | `tv` 2400, displayed 4900 (`disc` 2500), `lie_id` 7, `leak_modes` 3 | `DELIBERATE` NOTE `LIE` |
| 5 | Same lie, leak dropped | as row 4, `leak_modes` 2 (GLIDE only) | `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` DEFECT |
| 6 | Lie that never renders | `tv` 2400, displayed 2400, `lie_id` 7, `ledger_active`, GLEAM set, `lie_age` 131 runs >= `reach_age` 120, `n_eq` 121 > 120 | `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` DEFECT |
| 7 | Uncaused difference | `tv` 1013, displayed 1010 dkPa, `lie_id` 0, not `ledger_active` | `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` DEFECT |
| 8 | Sanity/Filter skew | ledger row `ACTIVE` this run, `Perceived.lie_id` still 0, `n_skew` 5 <= 24 | `SKEW` NOTE |
| 9 | Skew that does not clear | as row 8 with `n_skew` 30 > 24 | `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` DEFECT (branch b: the Filter never picked the entry up) |
| 10 | Sanity `PENDING` row | the ledger row is `PENDING` (onset delay), `lie_id` 0, `tv` = displayed | `HONEST` OK (a `PENDING` row is not `ledger_active`; no lie exists yet) |
| 11 | Remote seat | viewer 2 on a one-seat build, or any viewer on a `MIRROR` | `UNAVAILABLE` / `REMOTE_VIEWER` or `NO_LEDGER`, NOTE |
| 12 | Probe present, bypass | drawn needle differs from the perceived-implied angle by 0.30 deg > `LEAK_EPS_DEG` 0.25 | `DEFECT_BYPASS` DEFECT (at exactly 0.25 deg the probe check passes: inclusive) |

**Worked example: three returns** (Graph `hp` 0 to 10000 bp, needle `arc_deg` 240 (Instruments IW rule at in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md:201; also the Filter's own example at pf:648), so 1 deg = 41.67 bp; the Filter's `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` 3.0 deg/s = 125 bp/s, and 75 bp/s at `rm` 0.6; a 60-deg discrepancy at removal is 2500 bp, which the Filter's D.6 example gives as 20 s, and 33.3 s under reduced motion):

1. *Honest return, static true value.* `tv` 2400 held, lie removed with displayed 4900 (`disc_end` 2500 bp). At `t_ret` 1.0 s (inside `WANDER_BLEND_S`) the displayed value is 4775 plus an illustrative +12 bp wander residue, so `disc_t` = 2387; envelope test: 2387 <= 2500 + 0 + 250 = 2750 holds. (The Filter's wander term fades out over `WANDER_BLEND_S` and can move the displayed value by more than the follower's rate alone, so a per-step test would be unsafe in this window; the envelope is anchored on the removal instead.) At `t_ret` 6.0 s (past `WANDER_BLEND_S`, so the step test applies) the displayed value is 4150, `disc_t` 1750; at the previous 10 Hz refresh (5.9 s) the displayed value was 4162.5, rounded half up to 4163, so `disc_prev` 1763: 1750 <= 1763 + 0 + 250 holds. `RETURNING` NOTE throughout; at `t_ret` 20.0 s `disc` is 0 and the class becomes `HONEST`. The same lie at `rm` 0.6 takes 33.3 s, inside the Filter's published `t_return_max` for that lie.
2. *Moving true value.* The same return with the node degrading, `tv` falling at 200 bp/s while the follower closes at 125 bp/s: the discrepancy **grows** by 75 bp/s = 7.5 bp per refresh. Step test at 10 Hz: `disc_t` = `disc_prev` + 7.5 <= `disc_prev` + abs(`tv_t` - `tv_prev`) (20 bp) + 250. `RETURNING` holds, correctly: a follower cannot close faster than the truth moves away. (A step test without the `abs(tv_t - tv_prev)` term would flag this honest return.)
3. *Stuck return.* The displayed value freezes at 4150 (`disc` 1750) at `t_ret` 6.0 s and stays. The step test passes (a frozen discrepancy does not grow), so the **time bound** catches it: by `t_ret` 33.0 s the return has outlasted the 96-deg cap bound at `rm` 1.0 (32 s; under reduced motion it would be caught at 53.4 s; the per-lie `t_return_max` is normally tighter), the row fails and the class becomes `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`. A return whose `disc` grows beyond `disc_prev` + `abs(Delta tv)` + 250 fails at once. A binary-frame ref (a cracked-housing `HOUSING_GLOW`) in `RETURN` with `disc` 1 fails at once: the frame cut back at removal (pf:778).

**Worked example: silent-lie timing in runs.** A 40 s lie: `end_run - active_run` = 200 runs (Sanity runs at 5 per second); `reach_age` = ceil(600 x 200 / 1000) = 120 runs = 24 s. A lie that is not rendered reads `DELIBERATE` tagged `ONSET` for the first 24 s, however many frames `disc <= tol_eff`; from 24 s the `n_eq` counter (zero until then) must exceed `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES` 120 (2 s at 60 fps) before `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` fires, at about 26 s. A slow `GLIDE` onset (departure rate as low as 0.3 to 0.5 deg/s, so `disc` stays inside `tol_eff` for many seconds on an honest lie) therefore never produces a false defect: by the reach age the Filter guarantees arrival at `D_eff >= D_VIS_MIN_DEG` (pf:646).

### F-2. `graph_hook_sum_run`

The `graph_hook_sum_run` formula is defined as:

`graph_hook_sum_run(r) = t(graph.propagate.power, r) + t(graph.propagate.atmo, r) + t(graph.propagate.failure, r)`

The Graph's budget is a P99 of the **sum of three hooks per run** while the kernel publishes only per-hook `t_hook`, so the Overlay folds the three into one sample per Graph run. A run is a tick with `T mod 6 == 0` (the three hooks have divisor 6, offset 0 and are chained by `after:`, so they run in the same tick, in that order).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t(h, r)` | int microseconds | 0 to unbounded | The kernel's per-hook `t_hook` for hook `h` in run `r`, from the kernel's diagnostics timer (the one allow-listed wall-clock reader, H9, kern:781); the Overlay reads no clock of its own | One hook's time in Graph run `r` |
| `h` | hook id | exactly `graph.propagate.power`, `graph.propagate.atmo`, `graph.propagate.failure` | kernel resolved order (kern:607) | The bare string `graph.propagate` never appears |

**Output Range:** `graph_hook_sum_run` >= 0 us per run; displayed in ms to two decimals. Never `DEFECT`: the figure is a dev-template number and the budget it is compared with is defined on the Release export template (DO-19). **Example:** 120 + 150 + 40 = 310 us = 0.31 ms (power, atmo, failure). `step_over_budget` (kern D.6's own example, 1.2 + 0.9 + 0.4 + 0.3 + 0.1 = 2.9 ms against `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4) is displayed from the kernel, not recomputed.

### F-3. `hook_window_p99`

The `hook_window_p99` formula is defined as:

`hook_window_p99 = S[ ceil_div(99 x n, 100) ]`, where `S` is the ascending sort of the last `n = min(N, runs seen)` values of `graph_hook_sum_run`, `N = DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` (nearest-rank percentile, 1-indexed); `verdict = insufficient if n < DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES; WARN if hook_window_p99 > GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS.P99 or max(S) > GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS.max; else OK`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N` = `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` | int runs | 100 to 3600 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 600 (60 s of Graph runs at 10 per second) | Tuning Knobs | The rolling window length |
| `n` | int | 0 to `N` | model | Samples currently held; the window advances **per Graph run, not per rendered frame**, and does not advance while the kernel is `PAUSED` |
| `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` | int | 100 to 600 (at most `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS`) `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 100 | Tuning Knobs | The smallest `n` that yields a P99 verdict; a P99 over fewer than 100 samples would just be the maximum |
| `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` | ms | 0.5 P99 / 1.0 max per run (MVP) = 500 us / 1000 us; 1.0 P99 per tick (VS-A) | registry `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`, **cited verbatim**; defined on the **Release export template**, sum of the three `graph.propagate.*` hooks | The budget. The Graph's own row label `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_US` (lsg:724) is never surfaced as a second constant |

**Output Range:** `hook_window_p99` >= 0 us, one of the window's own samples (nearest rank never interpolates); `verdict` in {`OK`, `WARN`, `insufficient samples (n/N)`}; **never `DEFECT`** and always labelled "dev template: indicative" (the gate is the Graph's S1 spike and performance gate). The VS-A column of the registry (1.0 ms P99 **per tick**) is a different aggregation (per tick, not per run); it is a contract for later, and the MVP verdict uses the MVP column only. **Example:** `N` 600; per-run sums: 590 at 310 us, 6 at 420 us, 3 at 710 us, 1 at 1120 us. Rank = ceil_div(99 x 600, 100) = 594; sorted positions 1 to 590 are 310, 591 to 596 are 420, 597 to 599 are 710, 600 is 1120, so `S[594]` = 420 us <= 500 us (P99 `OK`); `max(S)` 1120 > 1000 (`WARN`, labelled indicative). With `n` 99 the verdict is `insufficient samples (99/100)`, never a number and never `OK`.

### F-4. `dirty_load_pm`

The `dirty_load_pm` formula is defined as:

`dirty_load_pm(b, T) = floor(1000 x keys(b, T) / MAX_DIRTY_KEYS)`; an `ALL` collapse sets `collapse(b, T) = true` and `dirty_load_pm = 1000`

A cascade that approaches `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` collapses to `ALL` (kern:106, D.9), so the Overlay shows how close each block comes per tick. The refresh folds the ticks since the last refresh into `last`, `peak` (max over the folded ticks) and `collapse_count` per block (DO-18).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `keys(b, T)` | int | 0 to 256, or `ALL` | The P5 notification batch `(block_id, tick, change_keys)` (CR-10) | Change keys for block `b` at tick `T` |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | int | 256 | registry `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, **never altered** | The collapse threshold |
| `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` | int per mille | 250 to 900 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 500 | Tuning Knobs | `WARN` at or above (inclusive) |
| `DIAG_DIRTY_ALERT_PM` | int per mille | `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` to 1000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 750 | Tuning Knobs | Pins the row to the top of the page; still `WARN`, no new severity |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1000 per mille per `(block, tick)`, and an `ALL` flag shown separately; an `ALL` collapse is `WARN`, never `DEFECT` (it is legal kernel behaviour; a restore produces one `ALL` then silence, annotated `post_restore`). **Example:** the Graph's measured worst cascades (lsg:201, LS-20): 38 keys (MVP) -> floor(38000 / 256) = **148** (`OK`); 127 keys -> 496 (`OK`); 128 keys -> **500** (`WARN`, inclusive boundary); 135 keys (VS-A) -> **527** (`WARN`); 192 keys -> **750** (`WARN`, pinned); an `ALL` collapse -> `collapse_count` + 1, `WARN`.

### F-5. `queue_load_pm`

The `queue_load_pm` formula is defined as:

`queue_load_pm = floor(1000 x depth / QUEUE_CAPACITY)`, and per issuer `queue_load_pm(i) = floor(1000 x depth(i) / PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY)`; `WARN if max over {total, issuers} >= DIAG_QUEUE_WARN_PM`

`queue_load_pm` is the Overlay's name, not the kernel's: the kernel publishes depths and `lag_ticks`; the Overlay derives the load and attaches a threshold (peak-held per refresh like every `DEFECT`- or `WARN`-capable counter, DO-18). The catch-up lag is cited, not re-derived: `lag_ticks` is the kernel's formula (kern:351; `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4), compared with `DIAG_LAG_WARN_TICKS`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `depth`, `depth(i)` | int commands | 0 to the capacity | Kernel diagnostics (total and per-issuer queue depth) | The current queue occupancy |
| `QUEUE_CAPACITY` | int | 1024 | registry `QUEUE_CAPACITY`, cited | Total queue capacity |
| `PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY` | int | 256 | registry `PER_ISSUER_CAPACITY`, cited | Per-issuer capacity |
| `DIAG_QUEUE_WARN_PM` | int per mille | 250 to 900 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 500 | Tuning Knobs | The `WARN` threshold (inclusive); 500 per mille is 512 commands total or 128 per issuer |
| `lag_ticks`, `DIAG_LAG_WARN_TICKS` | int ticks | 0 to unbounded; 1 to 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 4 | kernel (kern:351); Tuning Knobs | The simulated-time lag; `WARN` at or above the knob |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1000 per mille (the kernel never exceeds capacity: a full queue rejects with `QUEUE_FULL`, which is itself a `WARN` row on the command-log tail). **Example:** 5 commands queued in total -> floor(5000 / 1024) = **4** (`OK`); 512 -> **500** (`WARN`); 1024 -> 1000; one issuer holding 130 of 256 -> floor(130000 / 256) = **507** (`WARN`, worst issuer). Lag, from the kernel's own example: a 1 s hitch gives `lag_ticks` = 60 - 4 = **56** (`WARN`) after the first frame, repaid at 3 per frame.

### F-6. `ticks_per_overlay_refresh`

The `ticks_per_overlay_refresh` formula is defined as:

`ticks_per_overlay_refresh = TICK_RATE / DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, and `peak = max(sample_i)` over the ticks folded into one refresh

The kernel emits at `TICK_RATE` 60; numerals refresh at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, so several ticks fold into one displayed value. The refresh is triggered by the accumulated frame delta of the engine's process callback, not by a wall-clock read (DO-19; -> ADR candidate).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TICK_RATE` | int Hz | 60 | registry `TICK_RATE`, cited | The kernel rate |
| `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` | int Hz | 2 to 30 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 10 | Tuning Knobs | The numeral refresh rate; markers and debug draws update per rendered frame regardless |

**Output Range:** 2 to 30 ticks per refresh (non-integer when `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` does not divide 60; the quotient is informative and the model folds whatever ticks arrived). If the rendered frame rate is below `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` the effective refresh is the frame rate. **Example:** 60 / 10 = **6** ticks, equal to the Graph hooks' divisor, so each refresh folds exactly one Graph run; at 30 Hz it is 2; at 2 Hz it is 30; at 7 Hz it is 8.57 and some refreshes fold two Graph runs, which is why peak-hold is mandatory; at 8 fps and a 10 Hz setting, each frame refreshes and folds 60 / 8 = 7.5 ticks.

### F-7. `overlay_cpu_us`

The `overlay_cpu_us` formula is defined as:

`overlay_cpu_us = (t_copy_us x TICK_RATE + t_model_us x DIAG_REFRESH_HZ + t_view_us x fps) / fps  <=  OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US`

The Overlay must not distort the numbers it displays, so its own per-rendered-frame cost has a ceiling (dev builds only). The three terms are the handler copy per tick, the classifier and formatting per refresh, and the view update per rendered frame.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_copy_us` | int us | 0 to about 200 | **The kernel's diagnostics timer, exposed read-only (DO-33c, APPLIED by the kernel 2026-10-27)** (the Overlay reads no wall clock of its own, H9) | Handler copy per tick |
| `t_model_us` | int us | 0 to about 2000 | same | Classifier and formatting per refresh |
| `t_view_us` | int us | 0 to about 1000 | same | View update per rendered frame |
| `fps` | float | 20 to 240 | engine | Rendered frames per second |
| `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US` | int us | 250 to 2000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 1000 | Tuning Knobs | The dev-only ceiling (the same scale as `LIGHT_CPU_BUDGET_US` 1000) |

**Output Range:** >= 0 us per rendered frame, averaged over `DIAG_SELF_COST_WINDOW_FRAMES`. Above budget the row is `WARN` and the view throttles along one refresh ladder, {`DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, floor(`DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`/2), 2} (10, 5, 2 Hz at the default): one rung down per over-budget window and one rung up after two consecutive under-budget windows `[PLACEHOLDER]` (E-DO-35); the gate is the **window mean** above, and the P99 and maximum are logged for information only; **collection is never skipped**, so a throttled Overlay still sees every tick. Dev builds only; wall-clock acceptance criteria are `[blocked: reference hardware]` (OQ-PF-25) and indicative until the technical director names two machines. **Example (illustrative targets, not measurements):** `t_copy_us` 30, `t_model_us` 400, `t_view_us` 150 at 60 fps: (30 x 60 + 400 x 10 + 150 x 60) / 60 = (1800 + 4000 + 9000) / 60 = **246.7 us** <= 1000 (`OK`).

### F-8. `hash_cost_per_s`

The `hash_cost_per_s` formula is defined as:

`hash_cost_per_s = (TICK_RATE / DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS) x t_hash_ms`; amortised per tick `= t_hash_ms / DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS`; drift is caught within `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS / TICK_RATE` seconds

**An advisory illustration for the kernel amendment (DO-33b), not an Overlay gate:** the hash is the kernel's cost and the Overlay never schedules or performs it.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_hash_ms` | float ms | 0.1 to about 5 | Save/Load OQ-17 (sl:1316): a GDScript 64-bit hash over 100 to 300 KiB may exceed 1.5 ms | One `state_hash` (D.12, `H64` pending ADR) |
| `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 600 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, a **kernel knob (accepted 2026-10-27, command-simulation-layer.md Tuning Knobs)**, default 60 | Tuning Knobs (kernel owner) | The kernel's hash cadence |
| `TICK_RATE` | int Hz | 60 | registry, cited | The kernel rate |

**Output Range:** 0 to about `TICK_RATE x t_hash_ms` ms per real second. **Example** with `t_hash_ms` 1.5: cadence 1 (per tick, kern:915's wording) = 60 x 1.5 = **90 ms per second**, 9 percent of wall time, and each 1.5 ms spike would be 37.5 percent of `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 if it landed in P3; cadence 6 = 15 ms/s; cadence 60 (default) = **1.5 ms/s** (0.025 ms per tick), drift caught within 1 s; cadence 600 = 0.15 ms/s, drift caught within 10 s.

### F-9. `stage_entry_latency_s`

The `stage_entry_latency_s` formula is defined as:

`stage_entry_latency_ds = floor((T_enter - T_mark) x 10 / TICK_RATE)`, shown as `stage_entry_latency_s = stage_entry_latency_ds / 10`

AC-SN-88 (sn:1626) requires reading the stage-1 entry window (268 to 363 s of boarding) off the Overlay: the Overlay supplies the stopwatch, Sanity supplies the window and owns the verdict.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_mark` | int ticks | 0 to run length | The observer's `mark` action (DO-31), the tick of boarding | The stopwatch start |
| `T_enter` | int ticks | `T_mark` to run length | The kernel tick at which the Overlay's refresh first reads `sanity_view(player_id).stage` as 1 after 0 (the refresh tick, so the reading is up to `ticks_per_overlay_refresh` ticks late: 6 ticks, 0.1 s, at the default 10 Hz; *orchestrator ruling*: the Overlay subscribes to no Sanity change notification) | The stage-1 entry |

**Output Range:** >= 0, in tenths of a second; no severity (Sanity's AC owns the 268 to 363 s window). **Example:** mark at T = 600, stage-1 entry observed at T = 19,560 -> floor(18,960 x 10 / 60) = 3160 tenths = **316.0 s**, inside 268 to 363 s (Sanity's own calibration hypothesis is 315.8 s, sn:344).

## Edge Cases

Format: *(E-DO-nn)* ids are cited by the rules, the open questions and the acceptance criteria. Hook ids in examples are the real ones only (`graph.propagate.power`, `graph.propagate.atmo`, `graph.propagate.failure`, `sanity.drift`). Every outcome is a display outcome: no edge case ever causes the Overlay to write, submit, force or repair anything.

**A. Kernel states and boundaries**

- **If the kernel fails to boot because a hook declares an unknown `after:` id or a cycle** *(E-DO-01)*: the kernel is `FAULTED` from `BOOTING` and never ticks; the Overlay (drawn despite no ticks, DO-11) shows the boot progress, the offending `hook_id` or the cycle path, and the FAULT record `(T, type, issuer, host_order, block_id, assertion)` with absent fields shown as `n/a`; every ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `NOT_RUNNING`. The Overlay is the tool one reaches for when the kernel is least healthy (kern:606).
- **If the kernel enters `FAULTED` mid-run in a dev build** *(E-DO-02)*: `T` stays frozen, the FAULT record is pinned on `P-KERNEL` and on the strip, the other pages freeze on the last good views, page changes still work, and nothing is submitted. In `HIDDEN` the record is **not** drawn (a tester must not see it either, DO-11); `reveal` shows it on the strip. In a release build the Overlay does not exist and the kernel returns to the menu.
- **If the kernel is `PAUSED`** *(E-DO-03)*: `T` is frozen and numerals still refresh so a paused session is inspectable; the kernel's paused service cycle (CR-16: no P3, `T` unchanged) can still deliver P5 batches, which the Overlay counts in the dirty-key `last` figure but folds into no hook window; the hook window, the `n_eq` / `n_skew` counters and `t_ret` freeze (`t_ret` counts ticks), so a long pause cannot accrue silent-lie time. A pause to read a defect therefore never creates one.
- **If a restore, new world or transit completes while the Overlay is open** *(E-DO-04)*: on the `LOADING` to `RUNNING` edge every history window (P99 samples, peaks, silent-lie and skew counters, `RETURN` trackers) is cleared; for `DIAG_POST_RESTORE_SETTLE_FRAMES` rendered frames after the first `RUNNING` tick every ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `SETTLING`, so the Filter's presentation-local tails (a return track dropped by the restore, E-PF-5) never read as `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`; the one `ALL` collapse that follows is shown `WARN` annotated `post_restore`; `hash_T` equals the restore tick (the forced restore hash, SL-12). Opening the Overlay mid-session starts empty windows and says "insufficient samples (n/N)" rather than issuing a verdict.
- **If the kernel is `SAVING`** *(E-DO-05)*: all pages stay live and the Save/Load channel's row shows `save_status` (`WRITING`, then `WRITTEN` with `written_at_T`); a `FAILED` status is a `DEFECT` row naming the last `SL_*` reason. The Overlay never delays or retries a save (display only).
- **If the Overlay's dev tag is absent or its root is missing from an export** *(E-DO-06)*: the composition root registers no `DIAGNOSTIC` subscription and no dev action, the Overlay root scene is never instantiated, and the CI file-list check fails the build if any `dev_artifact_list` path is present in a release export. There is no runtime branch to flip (DO-2), so a player cannot obtain the oracle by any setting.

**B. The classifier and the Sanity/Filter join**

- **If two `ACTIVE` ledger entries target one subject for the viewer** *(E-DO-07, PF-46)*: the ref keeps one row and one class, taken from the entry with the lowest `(start_run, slot)`; the Filter draws the lowest `(start_run, lie_id)` (PF-46, pf:467), so on a `start_run` tie the Overlay's pick for `end_run` and `reach_age` may be a different lie from the one drawn: the row shows the Filter's `lie_collisions` and a "tie: pairing unverified" note until OQ-DO-1 settles `lie_id` provenance. Two rows or two classes for one ref would let one entry's `DELIBERATE` hide the other's defect.
- **If the Filter's `lie_id` and Sanity's `slot` do not identify the same lie, or the Filter's `lie_id` is minted by a different rule than the ledger assumes** *(E-DO-08, OQ-DO-1)*: nothing changes, because the join is by `(viewer, subject_ref)` and never by `lie_id`; the Filter's `lie_id` is displayed as the Filter reports it and is used only for the `lie_id != 0` presence tests. A provenance change on either side cannot silently break the classifier.
- **If `lie_id` stays non-zero during `RETURN`, or is 0 throughout** *(E-DO-09, OQ-DO-2)*: the classes are unchanged. `SKEW` counts `lie_id != 0` only while `motion_mode != RETURN`; `DEFECT_NO_LEAK` excludes `RETURN` (the return amplitude fades to 0 at `LEAK_EPS_DEG`, so a dropped GLEAM is expected at the end); `RETURNING` tests `motion_mode == RETURN` and not `ledger_active` with no `lie_id` test. The Filter's confirmed behaviour (`lie_id` kept through `RETURN`, 0 once the follower closes; registry `filter_counters`) cannot flip a verdict.
- **If the Filter has not published `(value_lo, value_hi)` for a ref (`q_display` unknown) and `tol(kind)` is 0 (`o2_pm`, `n_o2`, `n_inert`)** *(E-DO-10, OQ-DO-2, OQ-DO-3)*: `step_enabled` is false for that ref, the step sub-test of `RETURNING` is skipped, and the return is held only by `t_return_max(lie)`; if that is also unpublished the row shows "bound n/a" and a frozen or slow return is not flagged. A false `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` from one-per-mille rounding jitter is judged worse than a missed stuck return on three fields; `HONEST` stays exact equality either way.
- **If a binary-frame ref (`SPRITE_SWAP`, `STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`) is in `RETURN` with a non-zero `disc`** *(E-DO-11)*: the class is `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` at once. The frame cuts back to the honest frame at removal (pf:778); only the gleam decays over the Filter's 6.6 s, and a gleam is not a value discrepancy, so `disc` must already be 0.
- **If the first `WANDER_BLEND_S` seconds of an honest return move the displayed value by more than the follower's rate** *(E-DO-12)*: the envelope test (`disc_t <= disc_end + abs(tv_t - tv_end) + tol_eff`) holds, because it is anchored on the removal and not on the previous refresh; the step test starts only at `t_ret >= WANDER_BLEND_S` (cited from the Filter, 3 s). A per-step test across the fade-out would false-flag the wander term as a growing discrepancy.
- **If a return outlasts `t_return_max(lie)`, grows beyond the step or envelope bound, or freezes** *(E-DO-13)*: crossing `t_return_max` or growth past the bound makes the class `DEFECT_UNCAUSED`; a frozen discrepancy passes the step test (it does not grow) and is caught only by the time bound. `t_return_max` is per lie and scales with the latched `rm` (32 s at 1.0, 53.3 s at 0.6 for the 96-deg cap), so a reduced-motion player's honest 40 s return is never a defect.
- **If the Sanity row is `PENDING` (the 2 to 5 s onset delay), or has just left `ACTIVE` for `EXPIRED`, `DEBUNKED` or `INVALIDATED`** *(E-DO-14)*: a `PENDING` row is not `ledger_active` and no lie exists yet, so a true-equals-displayed ref reads `HONEST`, never a defect; an ended row is not active either, and the three end types are classified identically (`RETURNING`), because the Filter renders them identically (no relief cue, SN-16). The ledger row's `state` is shown beside the class so the operator still sees which end it was.
- **If a lie begins or ends between two numeral refreshes** *(E-DO-15)*: the per-frame marker updates in that frame; the numerals and the class follow within `1 / DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` s; for at most `DIAG_SKEW_GRACE_FRAMES` rendered frames the ledger and the `Perceived` record may disagree on presence, and that window reads `SKEW`. No frame shows an active lie with a stale `HONEST` tag for longer than one refresh interval.
- **If a `GLIDE` lie departs very slowly (a rate as low as 0.3 to 0.5 deg/s) so `disc` stays inside `tol_eff` for many seconds** *(E-DO-16)*: the class is `DELIBERATE` tagged `ONSET` until the lie age reaches `ceil(GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x (end_run - active_run))` runs; only then does the `n_eq` counter run toward `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES`. A grace measured in frames alone would flag every honest slow onset.
- **If a repair makes the true value equal the lie's displayed target while the entry is still `ACTIVE`** *(E-DO-17)*: `disc` drops inside `tol_eff` for a moment; Sanity invalidates the lie at the next run when the true state equals the displayed state (sn:393, EC-SN-37), about 12 frames, which is inside the 120-frame grace, so no `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` fires. Were the entry to persist beyond the grace, the defect would be correct: a lie that renders as the truth has no seam.
- **If a new lie starts on a ref that is in `RETURN`** *(E-DO-18)*: the Filter starts it from its own formula (pf:502); the ledger row is `ACTIVE` again, so the ref leaves `RETURNING` for `DELIBERATE` / `ONSET`, and `t_ret`, `n_eq` and `n_skew` reset when the earlier lie ended. Two lies never share a counter.
- **If the ref is a binary-frame surface** (`STATE_GLYPH`, `HOUSING_GLOW`, `MACHINE_FRAME`, or a `SPRITE_SWAP` lie) *(E-DO-19)*: `disc` is 0 or 1, `tol` is 0 and `q_display` is not applied; `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` fires when the displayed state equals the true state past the age gate and the grace. The age gate is `SWAP_LEAD_S` x 5 runs for every lie kind drawn on such a surface: `SPRITE_SWAP`, and a `FALSE_OK` or `FALSE_FAIL` drawn on a housing appearance frame (pf:82, PF-19); *orchestrator ruling*: they share the Filter's swap lead, so one age gate. A `FALSE_OK` or `FALSE_FAIL` on a needle or readout is not binary: it departs toward the zone at the per-lie rate (PF-13, D.3 `target_pm`), is a STALE readout (PF-18), and uses `reach_age` and `tol_eff`.

**C. Viewer, co-op and ownership hooks**

- **If the selected viewer is not the local seat** *(E-DO-20)*: the Overlay never calls `perceived` for it (the Filter answers the local seat only and returns the honest record for a remote `PlayerId` while counting `remote_queries`, PF-10); the ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `REMOTE_VIEWER` and `P-TRUTH` reads "perceived: not queried". `remote_queries` therefore stays 0 in MVP and remains a pure leakage indicator.
- **If the build is a `MIRROR` (Vertical Slice B) and the viewer is its own seat** *(E-DO-21)*: the true/perceived pair is displayed, and the ref classifies `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_LEDGER`, because `sanity.ledger` is `HOST_ONLY` and `ledger_active` cannot be evaluated; the pair is never judged `HONEST` or `DELIBERATE`. `HOST_MIRROR_MISMATCH` stays `n/a` (DO-34).
- **If the viewer is switched mid-lie** *(E-DO-22)*: every per-`(ref, viewer)` counter and tracker resets, and the next refresh reads `(tv, p, ledger)` for the new viewer only, so no row mixes two viewers; the viewer id is always on screen. Addressing is by `PlayerId`, never `peer_id`.
- **If the ref is `SCANNER`** *(E-DO-23)*: no true source is published, so the class is `UNAVAILABLE` / `NO_TRUE_SOURCE` (OQ-DO-2); it is never `HONEST` and never counted as clean in a page's severity roll-up.
- **If a ref has no registered resolver for the subject (`NO_RESOLVER`), or the render probe is absent** *(E-DO-24)*: the first is `UNAVAILABLE` and shows the reason; the second leaves the `perceived` / `true_value` / ledger classes untouched and reads `bypass_check` = `ABSENT`, which is never shown as clean and never promoted to `UNAVAILABLE` on its own (DO-22).
- **If `tv` and `perceived` would straddle a tick boundary** *(E-DO-25)*: they cannot, because one refresh reads `tv` first, then `perceived` (a hit on the Filter's per-`(ref, viewer, frame)` memo), then the ledger row and counters, before any other callback (DO-20); a read split across a tick would otherwise show a false `DEFECT_UNCAUSED` on every tick boundary.
- **If more refs qualify than `DIAG_TRUTH_REFS_MAX`** *(E-DO-26)*: refs with `ledger_active` rank first, then refs the Filter was asked about, then pinned refs; the remainder is dropped from the list with a "+n not shown" line, never from the classifier's totals of `DEFECT` rows on the page header.

**D. Counters, hashes and timings**

- **If a block's batch collapses to `ALL`** *(E-DO-27)*: `dirty_load_pm` reads 1000, `collapse_count` increases and the row is `WARN`, never `DEFECT` (an `ALL` collapse is legal kernel behaviour, kern D.9); after a restore the one collapse is annotated `post_restore` and the following silence is expected.
- **If a one-tick spike (a 256-key batch, a 5 ms hook, a `QUEUE_FULL` reject) lands between two numeral refreshes** *(E-DO-28)*: it is peak-held, so the refresh shows the maximum over the 6 folded ticks (at 10 Hz) and the event cannot fall between samples; a row that clears itself without anyone reading it would hide the very event the page exists to catch.
- **If fewer than `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` Graph runs are in the window** *(E-DO-29)*: `hook_window_p99` shows `insufficient samples (n/N)`, never a number and never `OK`; the window advances per Graph run, so 99 samples need 9.9 s of `RUNNING` at 10 runs per second.
- **If `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` does not divide 60, or the rendered frame rate falls below it** *(E-DO-30)*: the model folds whatever ticks arrived (8.57 per refresh at 7 Hz; 7.5 per frame at 8 fps with a 10 Hz setting) and peak-hold keeps the figures honest; the effective refresh is the frame rate.
- **If `render_cpu_us` has fewer samples than `DIAG_FILTER_CPU_WINDOW_FRAMES`** *(E-DO-31)*: the median is shown as `insufficient samples (n/N)` with no verdict; once full it is compared with the registry's `CPU_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, a **median** per frame (AC-PF-43b), never per frame, so a single hitch is not a `WARN`.
- **If the kernel does not publish a hash cadence or a `hash_algo` field** *(E-DO-32, OQ-DO-5)*: the algorithm cell reads `H64 (pending ADR)`, the age of `state_hash` is shown with no severity, and a hash from a loaded save is compared only if both carry the same algorithm tag; with a tag mismatch (once the field exists) the compare is refused with `NOTE` "algo differs", never `HASH_DRIFT`.
- **If K-1 (the kernel's per-block cached hash) is absent, or an owner publishes no `block_hash()`** *(E-DO-33)*: the per-block column reads `n/a` (`NOTE`, not `DEFECT`) and `HASH_DRIFT` names no block; the Overlay never hashes a block itself, on a timer or on demand.
- **If a hook-window figure exceeds `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` on a dev build** *(E-DO-34)*: the row is `WARN`, labelled "dev template: indicative", and nothing more. The budget is defined on the Release export template where the Overlay is absent; the verdict belongs to the Graph's S1 spike and performance gate. The same holds for any capture taken with the Overlay `FULL`: its own cost (`overlay_cpu_us`) is in the number.
- **If the Overlay's own cost exceeds `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US`** *(E-DO-35)*: the self-cost row is `WARN`, the view throttles its refresh rate along one ladder, {`DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`, floor(`DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`/2), 2} (10, 5, 2 Hz at the default; never below the 2 Hz floor, the lower bound of the `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` range): one rung down at the end of each `DIAG_SELF_COST_WINDOW_FRAMES` window whose mean is over budget, and one rung up (the same ladder read in the other direction, capped at `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ`) after two consecutive windows under budget `[PLACEHOLDER]`, so a transient spike does not throttle the session for good; collection is never skipped, so a throttled Overlay still sees every tick, so `HIDDEN` and `FULL` remain equivalent in what they observe.
- **If a `dump` is written while the kernel is `PAUSED`** *(E-DO-36)*: the file is a pure function of the model, so two dumps at the same `T` are byte-identical; if `user://debug/` is unwritable the action shows a `NOTE` and the Overlay continues; beyond `DIAG_DUMP_MAX_FILES` the oldest dump is deleted, never an evidence file under `production/qa/evidence/`.

**E. Channels, presentation and use**

- **If a channel's `pull()` or a page render throws** *(E-DO-37)*: one `DEFECT` row names the `channel_id` and the error class, the other channels and pages are unaffected, and after `DIAG_PANEL_ERR_LATCH_N` consecutive failures the channel is `SUSPENDED` until the next mode change; the kernel stays `RUNNING` and the tick's `state_hash` is unchanged (kern:867).
- **If a channel registers late, never registers, or collides** *(E-DO-38)*: channel registration is accepted only while the kernel is `BOOTING`, a rule this GDD adopts by analogy with Kernel CR-6, which itself covers blocks, command types and hooks only, so a channel that misses it never appears and the expected-channel list (a static list: the 13 channels named in AC-DO-69, 15 with In-World Instruments and the HUD; Graph (true values on P-TRUTH, hook timings on P-HOOKS, `graph_*` counters and `block_hash()` on P-STATE), Perception Filter, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline, Sanity, Attention and Chart Table are read through dedicated pages or views, not registered channels; kept with the composition root's dev registration code, *orchestrator ruling*) shows `NOTE absent`; a channel first drawn before registration shows `PENDING`; a duplicate `channel_id`, an unknown row kind, a unit embedded in a row name or a severity word other than `OK`, `NOTE`, `WARN`, `DEFECT` is `REJECTED` with an error row, never a crash. Exceeding `DIAG_CHANNELS_MAX` or `DIAG_ROWS_PER_CHANNEL_MAX` is the same.
- **If a world debug draw uses a line width under 1 base pixel** *(E-DO-39)*: under 4.7 a line has no feather and vanishes, so every `draw_line` in the dev node uses at least `OVERLAY_DEBUG_DRAW_WIDTH_PX` 1 (pipe:923, `[REF breaking-changes.md]`).
- **If the layer-0 debug node is hidden by darkness, jolted by the camera or hue-graded at stage 3** *(E-DO-40, spike S-4)*: the draws are set unshaded in MVP and the spike measures their colours unchanged under `force_stage` 3; on failure the fallback is the layer-4 projection (OQ-DO-7), a Pipeline amendment request. The Overlay's world marks must not become corruptible by the lie they expose.
- **If the Attention phase is `CALM` and the TellAudit record is older than the last `tell_epoch`** *(E-DO-41)*: the TellAudit cell reads "last audit at T=..." and is `NOTE`, so a stale result is never read as current; any violation count above 0 is a `DEFECT` whatever the phase.
- **If the Overlay is `HIDDEN` and a plain action (`toggle`, `mode`, `page_next`, `page_prev`, `dump`, `mark`) arrives** *(E-DO-42)*: it is ignored, so a tester who presses F3 reveals nothing; only the `reveal` chord acts, and it leads to the strip, not `FULL`. `HIDDEN` stays hidden even on `FAULTED`. The residual risk is a process risk the code cannot remove: a tester's screen, recording or stream that shows `FULL` or the strip (it names the stage and phase) voids that session's data (X-BS-11, AC-BS-122).
- **If a designer or QA runs the Overlay `FULL` during a balance or perception playtest to "see the truth" and acts on it** *(E-DO-43, degenerate use)*: the session's findings about discoverability are void by the same oracle rule; the Overlay never gains a player-facing twin, and the fix for a lie that is only distinguishable with the Overlay open is a missing leak in the world (the design test), never a feature in the tool.
- **If the world's kernel `state_hash` differs with the Overlay on versus off over the same scripted run** *(E-DO-44)*: the Overlay is not shippable as a QA instrument; the replay acceptance criteria fail, the Overlay is excluded from the comparison harness until the leak (an allocation, a second `perceived` call that missed the memo, a retained block reference) is found. Inertness is the first acceptance theme.
- **If the player presses a key that matches a dev action in a release build** *(E-DO-45)*: nothing happens: the actions are not in the action map (DO-8) and the Overlay root is not in the export, so there is no hidden path and no accessibility or rebinding entry to find.
- **If a 4.6-style dual-focus change gives the Overlay keyboard focus** *(E-DO-46)*: non-interactive panels use `MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE` and the Overlay never takes gameplay focus (the 4.6 dual-focus and 4.5 recursive mouse-filter behaviour `[REF modules/ui.md]`), so a click through the Overlay area reaches the game and gameplay input is never consumed by a diagnostic page.
- **If the Overlay is open across a save and restore of the world** *(E-DO-47)*: its mode, page, viewer, marks and windows are dev-session state and are not in the snapshot or `state_hash` (DO-9); after the restore the mode and page persist for the session, every history is cleared, and nothing the Overlay holds can be restored into the world.

## Dependencies

Dependencies are bidirectional: for each row the "Reverse mention" column says whether the other GDD already names the Overlay (checked by search at authoring), so a missing mention is a stated request, not a silent gap. "Direction" reads from this GDD's side. The Overlay depends on every system it reads (a design-time dependency on the owner's published read view or channel, never a runtime call into the owner's logic); nothing depends on the Overlay at runtime, and nothing in a release build can, because the Overlay is not there (DO-2). The index lists three dependencies for this system (idx:98: Command & Simulation Layer, Perception Filter, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera); the remaining rows are reads-from relations the index leaves implicit under its "depends on everything" note for the Polish layer (idx:207). **Status vocabulary:** `designed` = locked in an earlier committed GDD; `designed (this run)` = on disk in the current orchestrated run, so its names are the owner's but may still move; `undesigned-provisional` = no file, the contract here is a request.

Accessor shape provisional (P-9) | designed (accessor shape provisional) | Yes: kern:203 row 11, kern:690, kern:749, kern:915-916 |

Provisional contracts for undesigned neighbours (P-1 and P-2 now designed (this run), kept for their accepted contracts)** (each a request this GDD makes; re-check when the neighbour lands; the reflexion log's rule is that a placeholder in a table of record becomes a fact unless marked and registered)

Not dependencies.** Failure & Trail System, Item & Module Database and Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits) have no Dependencies row: neither the first two nor this GDD mention each other (0 mentions), and Derelict Authoring has one generic preamble mention (derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md:653) that registers no interface (ruling: list them under "no contract" in Cross-References only).

**E. Engine dependencies (Godot 4.7.2).** The reference covers `CanvasLayer`, `Control`, `Label` and `RichTextLabel` (`modules/ui.md` `[REF]`), `draw_line` width (`breaking-changes.md` 4.7 `[REF]`) and `Time.get_ticks_usec` (`deprecated-apis.md` `[REF]`). It has **no** scripting, core, export or debugging file (this domain has none): export presets and resource filters, feature tags, `OS.has_feature`, `OS.is_debug_build`, the `Performance` singleton, `Viewport.get_render_info`, `EngineDebugger` and multi-window `Window` are all `[UNV, HIGH]`. Five spikes: **S-1** export file list (release-export an empty scene plus one file under the dev root, list the files); **S-2** feature-tag registration at the composition root (`OS.has_feature` in editor, dev export and release export); **S-3** an optional second window for the observer (not required for MVP); **S-4** world debug-draw colour measured unchanged under `force_stage` 3 (DO-26); **S-5** `Viewport.get_render_info` and `Performance` per-viewport draw-count fields on 4.7.2 (AC-DO-87; fallback: `P-FRAME` shows `n/a`).

**F. ADR candidates touching this system** (none exist; `docs/architecture/` holds only `tr-registry.yaml`): debug-gate / build-flag / export-filter (new, raised here); the diagnostics pipeline and export format (AT-32); the authority-model and tick-source ADR (kernel OQ 2); the H64 and K-1 hash ADR (sl:1316); the leak-transport ADR (pf:1848).

## Tuning Knobs

Every knob below is a **dev-session presentation knob** invented by this GDD: each is `[PLACEHOLDER]` (default, range and proposed registry name, final nowhere until a stage sets it), each is named `DIAG_*` or `OVERLAY_*`, and **none affects the simulation, a block, a snapshot or `state_hash`** (DO-9). The one exception is ★ `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS`, which is a **kernel knob (accepted 2026-10-27, command-simulation-layer.md Tuning Knobs)**: the kernel owner owns its value, its range and its home, and the Overlay depends on no value of it (DO-33). A ★ marks a cross-system fact that the registry-merge stage should hold; everything else is local to this tool and is registered only if the merge stage chooses to track dev knobs. Nothing here is exposed to a player (DO-30). The reflexion rule applies to every row: the value is a placeholder, never a fact.

| Knob (proposed registry name) | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DIAG_REFRESH_HZ` | 10 | Hz | 2 to 30 | Numerals unreadable, model cost up (`overlay_cpu_us`) | Laggy digits; single events missed unless peak-held (DO-18) | `ticks_per_overlay_refresh`, `OVERLAY_CPU_BUDGET_US`, peak-hold |
| ★ `DIAG_HASH_CADENCE_TICKS` (**kernel knob (accepted 2026-10-27, command-simulation-layer.md Tuning Knobs)**; source of truth: Command & Simulation Layer) | 60 | ticks | 6 to 600 | Drift found late (10 s at 600) | Hash cost dominates dev ticks (90 ms per real second at 1; F-8) | `hash_cost_per_s`, P-STATE age rule, kern:915 wording |
| `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS` | 600 | Graph runs | 100 to 3600 | A regression is diluted in the window | P99 noisy (a P99 over 100 runs is a single outlier) | `hook_window_p99`, `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` |
| `DIAG_P99_MIN_SAMPLES` | 100 | runs | 100 to 600 (at most `DIAG_HOOK_WINDOW_RUNS`) | Long wait for a first verdict | A false P99 over a handful of samples | `hook_window_p99` |
| `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` | 500 | per mille of `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 (500 = 128 keys) | 250 to 900 | A collapse arrives unwarned | `WARN` noise on ordinary cascades (the Graph's MVP worst is 148) | `dirty_load_pm`, `DIAG_DIRTY_ALERT_PM` |
| `DIAG_DIRTY_ALERT_PM` | 750 | per mille (192 keys) | `DIAG_DIRTY_WARN_PM` to 1000 | No pin before a collapse | Pin noise | `dirty_load_pm` (still `WARN`; no new severity) |
| `DIAG_SILENT_LIE_GRACE_FRAMES` | 120 | rendered frames (2 s at 60 fps) | 30 to 600 | A real silent lie is unflagged for many seconds past its reach age | False `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` while a repair-converged lie is still `ACTIVE` (up to one Sanity run, 12 frames) or while rounding jitter sits at the boundary | `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` (Filter), `tol_eff`, `n_eq`, DO-21 row 4 |
